The key challenge addressed is the reliance on Large Language Models (LLMs) for planning, which can be problematic due to their inherent knowledge limitations and potential inaccuracies. REPOA tackles this by incorporating three main components: (1) Adaptive Dependency Learning (ADL) for dynamically revising learned dependencies based on experience; (2) Fine-grained Failure-aware Operation Memory (FFOM) to avoid repeated failures by tracking past operation outcomes; and (3) Difficulty-based Exploration (DEX) to improve learning efficiency by strategically selecting easier and less-explored items as goals. The authors demonstrate REPOA's robustness and efficiency in Minecraft, showcasing its ability to acquire challenging late-game items that previous approaches struggled with. The experiments include ablation studies to evaluate the impact of each component, as well as testing under perturbed ground truth conditions to highlight the agent's ability to recover from inaccurate prior knowledge.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its holistic approach to addressing the challenge of planning in open-world environments with unreliable LLM knowledge. While individual components, like learning dependencies from experience, have been explored in prior works, the combination of adaptive learning, failure awareness, and difficulty-based exploration is novel. Specifically, the use of analogy and FFOM for revision is fairly new. The adaptive revision of incorrect LLM assumptions in a complex environment is less common than relying on fixed knowledge sources or fine-tuning. Compared to DECKARD, which is directly compared, REPOA is more adaptive as it revises its initial LLM predictions.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in the field of embodied AI: how to enable agents to learn and plan effectively in complex, interactive environments without relying on pre-programmed knowledge or perfect models. The ability to recover from inaccurate LLM knowledge is particularly significant. The experiments demonstrate substantial improvements in performance compared to existing baselines, including success in acquiring late-game items in Minecraft, which had been previously unreachable. Furthermore, it is important that the method demonstrates strong capabilities utilizing a smaller, open-weight LLM.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   The components are well-motivated and clearly explained.
    *   The experimental results are thorough and comprehensive, with ablation studies that highlight the contribution of each component.
    *   The experiments under perturbed conditions provide valuable insights into the agent's robustness.
    *   The paper is well-written and easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on Minecraft as the sole testbed limits the generalizability of the findings. While Minecraft is a complex environment, the specific challenges and dynamics might not be fully representative of all open-world environments.
    *   The control strategy, STEVE-1, is a potential limitation. While the paper acknowledges STEVE-1's shortcomings, a broader analysis of the planner's performance when working with different controllers would be beneficial.
    *   The selection of the initial small set of human written plans appears to influence the overall performance. This might limit the truly 'from scratch' learning promise.
    *   Limited quantitative assessment of robustness. While qualitative analysis of aha moments is interesting, robustness analysis is not clearly defined, or quantitatively measured.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field by providing a practical and effective approach for building autonomous agents that can learn to plan in complex environments with imperfect knowledge. The approach could inspire new research on adaptive planning strategies, failure-aware learning, and efficient exploration techniques.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of embodied AI by addressing a key challenge: how to create robust and efficient planning agents in open-world environments, even with the limitations of LLMs. The novelty of combining ADL, FFOM and DEX makes the method effective compared to other approaches. The strengths of the experimental results and paper clarity outweigh the weaknesses. In summary, while the limited testbed and controller represents some minor limitations, the methods could substantially influence the field.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Threading Keyframe with Narratives: MLLMs as Strong Long Video Comprehenders
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24158v1)
- **Authors**: Bo Fang, Wenhao Wu, Qiangqiang Wu, Yuxin Song, Antoni B. Chan
- **Abstract**: Employing Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) for long video understanding remains a challenging problem due to the dilemma between the substantial number of video frames (i.e., visual tokens) versus the limited context length of language models. Traditional uniform sampling often leads to selection of irrelevant content, while post-training MLLMs on thousands of frames imposes a substantial computational burden. In this paper, we propose threading keyframes with narratives (Nar-KFC), a plug-and-play module to facilitate effective and efficient long video perception. Nar-KFC generally involves two collaborative steps. First, we formulate the keyframe selection process as an integer quadratic programming problem, jointly optimizing query-relevance and frame-diversity. To avoid its computational complexity, a customized greedy search strategy is designed as an efficient alternative. Second, to mitigate the temporal discontinuity caused by sparse keyframe sampling, we further introduce interleaved textual narratives generated from non-keyframes using off-the-shelf captioners. These narratives are inserted between keyframes based on their true temporal order, forming a coherent and compact representation. Nar-KFC thus serves as a temporal- and content-aware compression strategy that complements visual and textual modalities. Experimental results on multiple long-video benchmarks demonstrate that Nar-KFC significantly improves the performance of popular MLLMs. Code will be made publicly available.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Nar-KFC" (Narrating Keyframes Captured), a novel plug-and-play module designed to improve the long video understanding capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs).  Nar-KFC addresses the challenge of processing long videos with the limited context windows of MLLMs. It operates in two main stages: First, it selects keyframes using a customized greedy search algorithm, optimizing for both query relevance and frame diversity, formulated as a graph selection problem. Second, to bridge the temporal gaps caused by sparse keyframe sampling, it inserts textual narratives generated by a captioning model between the selected keyframes, maintaining temporal continuity. Experimental results across multiple long-video benchmarks demonstrate that Nar-KFC significantly improves the performance of popular MLLMs without requiring retraining.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper has moderate novelty. Formulating keyframe selection as a graph-based optimization problem that combines query relevance and frame diversity is a solid contribution.  Furthermore, interleaving these keyframes with narratives is a clever way to maintain temporal coherence within the constraints of LLM context windows. However, the individual components, such as using CLIP for visual embeddings or a captioning model for generating narratives, are not themselves novel. The core novelty lies in the combination of these techniques within the Nar-KFC framework.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical and pressing problem in the field: enabling LLMs to effectively process and understand long videos. The ability to process long videos opens up a range of new application areas for MLLMs, and makes their use for existing short-form problems more efficient. By demonstrating a training-free approach that boosts existing MLLMs, the paper provides a practical and accessible solution.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Strong Empirical Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough experimental evaluation across multiple benchmarks and MLLMs, demonstrating the general applicability and effectiveness of Nar-KFC.  The ablation studies are well-designed and provide valuable insights into the contributions of different components.
    * **Practicality:**  The Nar-KFC module is designed as a plug-and-play solution, making it easy to integrate with existing MLLMs without the need for computationally expensive retraining.
    * **Principled Approach:** The formulation of keyframe selection as a graph optimization problem with a clear objective function is well-motivated and grounded in theory.
    * **Addresses a Key Limitation:** The paper tackles the inherent context length limitations of current MLLMs, which is a significant bottleneck for long video understanding.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Dependency on Captioning Quality:** The quality of the textual narratives is crucial to the performance of Nar-KFC.  While the paper explores the use of different captioning models, the narratives generated may still be limited or potentially introduce hallucinated information.  The captions are also not necessarily perfectly coherent with each other.
    * **Greedy Search Limitations:** While the greedy search algorithm offers computational efficiency, it may not always find the globally optimal set of keyframes. The paper acknowledges this and provides comparisons to an IQP approach, however, it is still not as robust as exact methods would be. The evaluation includes a comparison that shows the proposed approach is within 1% of the best method, showing its value.
    * **Limited exploration of temporal modelling:** The paper mainly discusses preserving, not really modelling the temporal dimension. Future explorations can include temporal encoders that can better model the long range temporal relations of videos.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper's approach of combining keyframe selection with interleaved narratives is likely to influence future research on long video understanding. It provides a valuable framework for addressing the context length limitations of MLLMs and highlights the importance of both visual and textual modalities. The results suggest that methods like SlowFast-LLaVA, which explore modelling temporal data with different frame rates or streams, should explore better methods for frame-selection as well.

**Justification:**

While the individual components of Nar-KFC are not groundbreaking, the combination of query-aware and diverse keyframe selection with interleaved narratives is a well-engineered and effective solution for long video understanding. The solid empirical results and practical design make it a valuable contribution to the field. The identified weaknesses, such as the dependency on captioning quality and the limitations of the greedy search algorithm, provide clear directions for future research.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### LKD-KGC: Domain-Specific KG Construction via LLM-driven Knowledge Dependency Parsing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24163v1)
- **Authors**: Jiaqi Sun, Shiyou Qian, Zhangchi Han, Wei Li, Zelin Qian, Dingyu Yang, Jian Cao, Guangtao Xue
- **Abstract**: Knowledge Graphs (KGs) structure real-world entities and their relationships into triples, enhancing machine reasoning for various tasks. While domain-specific KGs offer substantial benefits, their manual construction is often inefficient and requires specialized knowledge. Recent approaches for knowledge graph construction (KGC) based on large language models (LLMs), such as schema-guided KGC and reference knowledge integration, have proven efficient. However, these methods are constrained by their reliance on manually defined schema, single-document processing, and public-domain references, making them less effective for domain-specific corpora that exhibit complex knowledge dependencies and specificity, as well as limited reference knowledge. To address these challenges, we propose LKD-KGC, a novel framework for unsupervised domain-specific KG construction. LKD-KGC autonomously analyzes document repositories to infer knowledge dependencies, determines optimal processing sequences via LLM driven prioritization, and autoregressively generates entity schema by integrating hierarchical inter-document contexts. This schema guides the unsupervised extraction of entities and relationships, eliminating reliance on predefined structures or external knowledge. Extensive experiments show that compared with state-of-the-art baselines, LKD-KGC generally achieves improvements of 10% to 20% in both precision and recall rate, demonstrating its potential in constructing high-quality domain-specific KGs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LKD-KGC, a novel framework for constructing domain-specific Knowledge Graphs (KGs) without relying on predefined schemas or external knowledge. The framework leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to analyze document repositories, infer knowledge dependencies, determine optimal document processing sequences, and generate entity schemas. It then extracts entities and relationships based on the generated schema to construct the KG.  The authors evaluate LKD-KGC on several domain-specific datasets, demonstrating improvements in precision and recall compared to existing LLM-based KGC methods. The key innovation lies in its unsupervised approach to schema induction and hierarchical knowledge access within document repositories.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper exhibits moderate novelty. While LLMs have been used for KGC before, LKD-KGC's unsupervised, dependency-aware schema construction approach is a significant departure from schema-guided or reference-knowledge-integrated methods. The idea of processing documents in a specific order to build up knowledge context is intuitive but not commonly exploited. The autoregressive schema generation process appears to be unique.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is substantial. The ability to automatically construct domain-specific KGs without manual schema definition is highly valuable. It addresses a key bottleneck in KGC and opens up possibilities for applications in areas where domain knowledge is complex and internal. The reported improvements in precision and recall are noteworthy and practically relevant. The framework offers adaptability by utilizing summaries and vector retrieval to stay within context length constraint.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Unsupervised Approach:** Avoids the limitations of schema-guided approaches.
    *   **Dependency-Aware Processing:** Processes information in a knowledge order.
    *   **Autoregressive Schema Generation:** Builds hierarchical context and eliminates the need for external knowledge sources.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** Thoroughly evaluated on various datasets with different LLMs.
    *   **Addresses a Practical Problem:** Automates KGC for complex, private domain data.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on LLMs:** While positioned as a strength, it's also a limitation. The quality of the KG relies on the reasoning abilities and context window size of the underlying LLM.
    *   **Evaluation Limitations:** While using LLM-as-judge addresses lack of ground truth, its reliability is not absolute. Human annotation of a subset of triples would strengthen the validation.
    *   **Limited Generalizability:** While demonstrated across several domain specific datasets it lacks tests on more general and publically available datasets.
    *   **Reproducibility:** While code and documentation has been provided in the anonymous repository this is not enough to completely reproduce the results.

*   **Potential Influence:** LKD-KGC has the potential to significantly influence the field by offering a practical solution for KGC in complex, domain-specific settings. Future research may build on its dependency-aware processing and schema generation techniques. It could encourage the development of more sophisticated LLM-based KGC methods.

*   **Score Rationale:** LKD-KGC presents a reasonably novel and significant advancement in domain-specific KG construction. The unsupervised approach and dependency-aware processing address existing challenges. The empirical evaluation supports the claim of improved performance. While the LLM dependency and reliance on the generated triples for validation are weaknesses, the overall contribution justifies a score of 7. It provides a solid foundation for future research in automating and improving KGC for complex domain-specific data.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Mixed-R1: Unified Reward Perspective For Reasoning Capability in Multimodal Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24164v1)
- **Authors**: Shilin Xu, Yanwei Li, Rui Yang, Tao Zhang, Yueyi Sun, Wei Chow, Linfeng Li, Hang Song, Qi Xu, Yunhai Tong, Xiangtai Li, Hao Fei
- **Abstract**: Recent works on large language models (LLMs) have successfully demonstrated the emergence of reasoning capabilities via reinforcement learning (RL). Although recent efforts leverage group relative policy optimization (GRPO) for MLLMs post-training, they constantly explore one specific aspect, such as grounding tasks, math problems, or chart analysis. There are no works that can leverage multi-source MLLM tasks for stable reinforcement learning. In this work, we present a unified perspective to solve this problem. We present Mixed-R1, a unified yet straightforward framework that contains a mixed reward function design (Mixed-Reward) and a mixed post-training dataset (Mixed-45K). We first design a data engine to select high-quality examples to build the Mixed-45K post-training dataset. Then, we present a Mixed-Reward design, which contains various reward functions for various MLLM tasks. In particular, it has four different reward functions: matching reward for binary answer or multiple-choice problems, chart reward for chart-aware datasets, IoU reward for grounding problems, and open-ended reward for long-form text responses such as caption datasets. To handle the various long-form text content, we propose a new open-ended reward named Bidirectional Max-Average Similarity (BMAS) by leveraging tokenizer embedding matching between the generated response and the ground truth. Extensive experiments show the effectiveness of our proposed method on various MLLMs, including Qwen2.5-VL and Intern-VL on various sizes. Our dataset and model are available at https://github.com/xushilin1/mixed-r1.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Mixed-R1 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Mixed-R1, a unified framework for improving the reasoning capabilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) through reinforcement learning (RL). It addresses the issue that existing RL-based MLLM post-training often focuses on specific tasks, leading to limitations in generalizability.  Mixed-R1 comprises two key components: a high-quality mixed dataset called Mixed-45K, and a mixed reward function design, which includes various reward functions tailored to different MLLM tasks (binary answer, chart analysis, grounding, and open-ended responses). A novel open-ended reward, Bidirectional Max-Average Similarity (BMAS), is proposed to handle the complexities of long-form text responses by leveraging token embedding matching.  The authors conduct experiments to demonstrate the effectiveness of Mixed-R1 on various MLLMs, including Qwen2.5-VL and Intern-VL, showcasing improved performance on several reasoning benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its unified approach to RL-based MLLM post-training. It presents a framework capable of handling diverse tasks with a single training pipeline, using a specifically designed mixed dataset and reward system. The BMAS reward function for open-ended generation also represents a novel contribution, offering a tokenizer-based alternative to judge-LLMs used in other works.

*   **Significance:** Addressing the task-specific nature of RL-based MLLM training is significant. By providing a more general method, the authors address a crucial limitation and potentially make RL more accessible for improving MLLMs. The performance gains demonstrated on various benchmarks, particularly for the Qwen2.5-VL models, suggest practical benefits of the approach. The dataset, while relatively small (45K), could be useful for future research and comparisons.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Unified Framework:** The framework's ability to handle various tasks under a single pipeline is a significant strength, potentially simplifying the MLLM training process.
    *   **Novel Reward Function (BMAS):** The BMAS reward function is a resourceful solution to the difficult problem of rewarding high-quality generation, by leveraging the model's output. It avoids the need for an additional large model for judging and potentially reduces resource consumption.
    *   **Empirical Validation:**  The comprehensive experiments and ablation studies offer strong evidence to support the effectiveness of the Mixed-R1 framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset Size:** While high-quality, the Mixed-45K dataset might be considered relatively small compared to datasets used in MLLM pretraining or fine-tuning. A larger dataset might further improve performance.
    *   **Dependency on DeepSeek-R1 Framework:** The work heavily relies on the DeepSeek-R1 training pipeline. While this builds on existing knowledge, it reduces the overall methodological originality to some extent.
    *   **Limited Generalization Scenarios:** While the paper does demonstrate gains in multiple MLLM tasks, it's constrained to only image dataset experiments. Future efforts that cover video, speech and multiple input type benchmarks will be appreciated.

*   **Potential Influence:** The Mixed-R1 framework has the potential to influence future research in RL-based MLLM post-training by promoting more unified and versatile approaches. The novel BMAS reward function could also inspire new methods for evaluating and improving generative capabilities.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7**. The paper addresses a relevant issue in the field of multimodal learning and presents a well-designed, empirically validated framework with a novel reward function for long-form text generation. While the reliance on the DeepSeek-R1 framework and relatively small dataset size limit the overall novelty, the unified approach and practical benefits make it a significant contribution that can guide future research on MLLM training. Further effort to cover video, speech, and multiple input type benchmarks will be appreciated.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### SCOUT: Teaching Pre-trained Language Models to Enhance Reasoning via Flow Chain-of-Thought
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24181v1)
- **Authors**: Guanghao Li, Wenhao Jiang, Mingfeng Chen, Yan Li, Hao Yu, Shuting Dong, Tao Ren, Ming Tang, Chun Yuan
- **Abstract**: Chain of Thought (CoT) prompting improves the reasoning performance of large language models (LLMs) by encouraging step by step thinking. However, CoT-based methods depend on intermediate reasoning steps, which limits scalability and generalization. Recent work explores recursive reasoning, where LLMs reuse internal layers across iterations to refine latent representations without explicit CoT supervision. While promising, these approaches often require costly pretraining and lack a principled framework for how reasoning should evolve across iterations. We address this gap by introducing Flow Chain of Thought (Flow CoT), a reasoning paradigm that models recursive inference as a progressive trajectory of latent cognitive states. Flow CoT frames each iteration as a distinct cognitive stage deepening reasoning across iterations without relying on manual supervision. To realize this, we propose SCOUT (Stepwise Cognitive Optimization Using Teachers), a lightweight fine tuning framework that enables Flow CoT style reasoning without the need for pretraining. SCOUT uses progressive distillation to align each iteration with a teacher of appropriate capacity, and a cross attention based retrospective module that integrates outputs from previous iterations while preserving the models original computation flow. Experiments across eight reasoning benchmarks show that SCOUT consistently improves both accuracy and explanation quality, achieving up to 1.8% gains under fine tuning. Qualitative analyses further reveal that SCOUT enables progressively deeper reasoning across iterations refining both belief formation and explanation granularity. These results not only validate the effectiveness of SCOUT, but also demonstrate the practical viability of Flow CoT as a scalable framework for enhancing reasoning in LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SCOUT (Stepwise Cognitive Optimization Using Teachers), a fine-tuning framework designed to enhance reasoning in pre-trained language models (LLMs) by modeling recursive inference as a progressive trajectory of latent cognitive states, termed Flow Chain-of-Thought (Flow CoT).  SCOUT addresses limitations of existing CoT-based methods, which often rely on explicit intermediate reasoning steps or costly pre-training. The framework leverages progressive distillation, using teachers of increasing capacity to supervise each reasoning step, and a cross-attention-based retrospective module that integrates outputs from previous iterations without disrupting the model's inherent computation flow. Experiments on eight reasoning benchmarks demonstrate that SCOUT consistently improves accuracy and explanation quality compared to baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core novelty lies in the *Flow CoT* reasoning paradigm and its realization through the *SCOUT* framework. While recursive reasoning and knowledge distillation aren't novel concepts *per se*, the specific combination and the way they are applied here—progressive distillation coupled with the retrospective module to preserve the LLM's computational flow—are innovative.  The idea of treating each iteration as a distinct cognitive stage and aligning it with a teacher model of appropriate strength is also a notable contribution. This addresses the limitations of uniform supervision in previous recursive reasoning approaches. The lightweight nature of SCOUT, allowing fine-tuning without extensive architectural modifications or pre-training, is a practical advantage.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its potential to improve the reasoning capabilities of LLMs in a scalable and efficient manner. Existing CoT approaches require supervised data of reasoning traces, and pure recursive reasoning often requires pre-training. SCOUT offers an alternative path, enhancing reasoning *during fine-tuning*, making it more accessible and adaptable to various pre-trained models. The performance gains reported across multiple benchmarks support the practical value of the approach. Furthermore, the qualitative analysis provides insights into how SCOUT refines both belief formation and explanation granularity across iterations, suggesting deeper, more coherent reasoning.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear and well-defined problem statement:  The paper identifies clear limitations in existing CoT methods and presents a well-motivated alternative.
    *   Novel framework: The SCOUT framework is well-designed and addresses the identified challenges effectively.
    *   Solid experimental validation: Extensive experiments on diverse benchmarks demonstrate consistent performance improvements.
    *   Qualitative analysis: Provides valuable insights into the internal workings of the model and how it refines its reasoning process.
    *   Lightweight and practical:  The framework is designed to be lightweight and easily integrated into existing fine-tuning pipelines.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited exploration of alternative supervision strategies: The paper mainly focuses on progressive distillation. While effective, it could benefit from exploring other supervision methods at each step.
    *   Fixed number of reasoning steps:  Using a fixed number of iterations (T=3) is a limitation. Future work could explore adaptive iteration control, as the paper mentions.
    *   Limited architectural variations: The paper explores only limited architectural changes. The model partitioning is ablated, but other architectural improvements specific for recursive reasoning could be explored.
    *   While the code is mentioned as being integrated into existing fine-tuning workflows, releasing the code would greatly enhance reproducibility and impact.
* **Score Justification:**

The paper presents a strong contribution to the field of LLM reasoning. While it builds upon existing concepts like knowledge distillation and recursive processing, the novel combination and design of the SCOUT framework, particularly the Flow CoT paradigm, are noteworthy. The experimental results provide compelling evidence of its effectiveness, and the qualitative analysis offers valuable insights. While there are some limitations, as noted above, these do not significantly detract from the overall value of the work.  SCOUT has the potential to influence how LLMs are fine-tuned for reasoning tasks, making it a practical and scalable approach. Therefore, I assign a score of:

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Seeing is Not Reasoning: MVPBench for Graph-based Evaluation of Multi-path Visual Physical CoT
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24182v1)
- **Authors**: Zhuobai Dong, Junchao Yi, Ziyuan Zheng, Haochen Han, Xiangxi Zheng, Alex Jinpeng Wang, Fangming Liu, Linjie Li
- **Abstract**: Understanding the physical world - governed by laws of motion, spatial relations, and causality - poses a fundamental challenge for multimodal large language models (MLLMs). While recent advances such as OpenAI o3 and GPT-4o demonstrate impressive perceptual and reasoning capabilities, our investigation reveals these models struggle profoundly with visual physical reasoning, failing to grasp basic physical laws, spatial interactions, and causal effects in complex scenes. More importantly, they often fail to follow coherent reasoning chains grounded in visual evidence, especially when multiple steps are needed to arrive at the correct answer. To rigorously evaluate this capability, we introduce MVPBench, a curated benchmark designed to rigorously evaluate visual physical reasoning through the lens of visual chain-of-thought (CoT). Each example features interleaved multi-image inputs and demands not only the correct final answer but also a coherent, step-by-step reasoning path grounded in evolving visual cues. This setup mirrors how humans reason through real-world physical processes over time. To ensure fine-grained evaluation, we introduce a graph-based CoT consistency metric that verifies whether the reasoning path of model adheres to valid physical logic. Additionally, we minimize shortcut exploitation from text priors, encouraging models to rely on visual understanding. Experimental results reveal a concerning trend: even cutting-edge MLLMs exhibit poor visual reasoning accuracy and weak image-text alignment in physical domains. Surprisingly, RL-based post-training alignment - commonly believed to improve visual reasoning performance - often harms spatial reasoning, suggesting a need to rethink current fine-tuning practices.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MVPBench, a new benchmark for evaluating the visual physical reasoning abilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). The benchmark focuses on testing the models' ability to understand and reason about physical laws, spatial relations, and causality in complex visual scenes, going beyond simple image description. MVPBench is designed to rigorously assess visual chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning by requiring models to generate coherent, step-by-step reasoning paths grounded in visual evidence derived from multi-image inputs. It introduces a graph-based CoT consistency metric to verify the adherence of reasoning paths to valid physical logic.  The paper also explores the impact of Reinforcement Learning (RL) based alignment and shows that, contrary to expectations, it can sometimes harm spatial reasoning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the construction of the MVPBench dataset itself. Combining real-world visual physics, multi-image inputs, and multi-path CoT annotations is a significant contribution. Existing datasets often rely on simplified or synthetic visuals and lack the detailed reasoning step annotations. The introduction of the graph-based CoT metric is also a useful addition for evaluating reasoning fidelity. The counter-intuitive finding that RL alignment can sometimes hurt spatial reasoning presents a significant point for the community to consider, and potentially a direction for further research.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial gap in evaluating MLLMs. While models have shown impressive perceptual abilities, their ability to reason about the physical world, in a way that aligns with human understanding, remains limited. MVPBench provides a valuable tool for rigorously testing and improving this capability. The findings regarding RL alignment are significant as they question the current fine-tuning practices used to enhance visual reasoning performance.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The dataset is well-curated and spans diverse physical reasoning categories.
    *   The multi-image input format forces models to consider temporal context.
    *   The graph-based CoT metric provides a fine-grained evaluation of reasoning fidelity.
    *   The discovery that RL alignment can impair spatial reasoning is an important finding.
    *   Open-source benchmark provides access to all.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The evaluation relies on proprietary MLLMs (GPT-4, etc.), which limits control and transparency. This is acknowledged by the authors.
    *   While the dataset is comprehensive, it is still a limited sample of the vast complexity of real-world physics. Future efforts should explore expanding scene and domain coverage.
    *   The definition of ground-truth annotations may still be somewhat subjective, even with expert annotation.
    *   The reason as to why the RL can be detrimental is not explored in detail, and hence is left to be resolved in later investigations.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a significant influence on the field by:

    *   Providing a challenging and valuable benchmark for evaluating MLLMs.
    *   Highlighting the limitations of current models in visual physical reasoning.
    *   Inspiring new research directions in RL-based alignment for MLLMs, potentially focusing on vision-centric reward design.
    *   Focusing the attention of the community on visual grounding, causal structure, and explainable reasoning,

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field with a well-designed benchmark and intriguing findings. While limitations exist, and a wider discussion on negative consequences may be required, the paper addresses an important problem and provides valuable insights for future research. The MVPBench tool has the potential to substantially impact how the AI community measures "embodied intelligence" within vision language models.

### CodeV-R1: Reasoning-Enhanced Verilog Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24183v1)
- **Authors**: Yaoyu Zhu, Di Huang, Hanqi Lyu, Xiaoyun Zhang, Chongxiao Li, Wenxuan Shi, Yutong Wu, Jianan Mu, Jinghua Wang, Yang Zhao, Pengwei Jin, Shuyao Cheng, Shengwen Liang, Xishan Zhang, Rui Zhang, Zidong Du, Qi Guo, Xing Hu, Yunji Chen
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) trained via reinforcement learning with verifiable reward (RLVR) have achieved breakthroughs on tasks with explicit, automatable verification, such as software programming and mathematical problems. Extending RLVR to electronic design automation (EDA), especially automatically generating hardware description languages (HDLs) like Verilog from natural-language (NL) specifications, however, poses three key challenges: the lack of automated and accurate verification environments, the scarcity of high-quality NL-code pairs, and the prohibitive computation cost of RLVR. To this end, we introduce CodeV-R1, an RLVR framework for training Verilog generation LLMs. First, we develop a rule-based testbench generator that performs robust equivalence checking against golden references. Second, we propose a round-trip data synthesis method that pairs open-source Verilog snippets with LLM-generated NL descriptions, verifies code-NL-code consistency via the generated testbench, and filters out inequivalent examples to yield a high-quality dataset. Third, we employ a two-stage "distill-then-RL" training pipeline: distillation for the cold start of reasoning abilities, followed by adaptive DAPO, our novel RLVR algorithm that can reduce training cost by adaptively adjusting sampling rate. The resulting model, CodeV-R1-7B, achieves 68.6% and 72.9% pass@1 on VerilogEval v2 and RTLLM v1.1, respectively, surpassing prior state-of-the-art by 12~20%, while matching or even exceeding the performance of 671B DeepSeek-R1. We will release our model, training pipeline, and dataset to facilitate research in EDA and LLM communities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the CodeV-R1 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CodeV-R1, a reinforcement learning with verifiable reward (RLVR) framework designed to train large language models (LLMs) for automated Verilog (hardware description language) generation from natural language specifications.  The framework addresses three key challenges in applying RLVR to Verilog generation: the lack of automated verification, scarcity of high-quality training data, and the computational expense of RLVR. CodeV-R1 tackles these challenges by (1) developing a rule-based testbench generator for equivalence checking, (2) implementing a "round-trip data synthesis" method to generate high-quality NL-code pairs, and (3) employing a two-stage training pipeline: distillation followed by an adaptive variant of DAPO (Dynamic Sampling Policy Optimization) to reduce training cost. Their resulting model, CodeV-R1-7B, demonstrates improved performance on VerilogEval v2 and RTLLM benchmarks compared to existing state-of-the-art models. They will release the model, training pipeline, and dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel components integrated into a comprehensive framework. The rule-based testbench generator is significant because it provides a more reliable verification environment than current LLM-generated testbenches. The round-trip data synthesis technique is innovative for generating high-quality NL-code pairs, which is a known bottleneck in the field. The adaptive DAPO algorithm is a practical improvement to reduce RL training costs. Although the core idea of RLVR is not new (as other works applied this to code generation), the specific adaptations and optimizations for *Verilog* generation are novel and non-trivial. The theoretical justification for round-trip synthesis also provides a level of formalization not often seen in LLM-based code generation papers.

*   **Significance:** The work is significant because it makes Verilog generation, a crucial task in electronic design automation (EDA), more accessible through LLMs. Automating Verilog generation can potentially accelerate the design process and reduce the need for specialized hardware expertise. The improved performance demonstrated on standard benchmarks indicates that CodeV-R1 achieves tangible improvements over existing methods.  The open-sourcing of the model, dataset, and training pipeline has the potential to drive further research and development in this area within both the EDA and LLM communities. It contributes to a growing body of work showing how LLMs can be applied to practical engineering tasks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly identifies and addresses key challenges in applying RLVR to Verilog generation.
    *   The proposed solutions are well-motivated and technically sound.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate improved performance on established benchmarks.
    *   The open-sourcing of the model, dataset, and training pipeline promotes reproducibility and future research.
    *   The paper presents a clear theoretical framework to justify the data curation strategy.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper states comparisons against the 671B DeepSeek-R1, it does so using reported numbers and performs the comparisons after RL. It would have been more convincing to distill from this and perform RL.
    *   The results would be even more impactful if the Verilog generated were *synthesizable* without manual intervention. This presents significant challenge given the complex nature of synthesis, but is the ultimate goal.
    *   A limitation mentioned is that the testbench *probabilistically* improves consistency. This inherently limits the guarantees of the system. It would be beneficial to have some guarantees for testbench completeness
    *   While comparisons are made to RTLcoder, a more thorough comparison to the methodology differences and improvements may be beneficial
    *   A greater discussion around the limitations and scalability around testbench development and complexity would strengthen the claims of this work
    *   It might have been beneficial to include an ablation of only the RL components for an isolated comparison
    *   The dependence on DeepSeek-R1 for NL generation is a point of concern, as it is a closed-source model.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by providing a practical and effective framework for training LLMs for Verilog generation. The open-sourced resources could accelerate research in EDA and LLM communities, leading to further improvements in automated hardware design.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

CodeV-R1 is a valuable contribution to the field due to its innovative framework combining rule-based testbench generation, round-trip data synthesis, and adaptive RL training. The improved benchmark performance and open-source availability are major strengths. The main limitations are that it is a step towards functional correctness rather than synthesizability, it does rely on the black-box DeepSeek R1, and there is always going to be limited guarantees for the testbench.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Beyond Exponential Decay: Rethinking Error Accumulation in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24187v1)
- **Authors**: Mikhail L. Arbuzov, Alexey A. Shvets, Sisong Beir
- **Abstract**: The prevailing assumption of an exponential decay in large language model (LLM) reliability with sequence length, predicated on independent per-token error probabilities, posits an inherent limitation for long autoregressive outputs. Our research fundamentally challenges this view by synthesizing emerging evidence that LLM errors are not uniformly distributed but are concentrated at sparse "key tokens" ($5-10\%$ of total tokens) representing critical decision junctions. By distinguishing these high-impact tokens from the increasingly predictable majority, we introduce a new reliability formula explaining the sustained coherence of modern LLMs over thousands of tokens. Converging research streams reveal that long-context performance primarily depends on accurately navigating a few crucial semantic decision points rather than on uniform token-level accuracy, enabling targeted strategies that significantly outperform brute-force approaches. We thus propose a framework for next-generation systems centered on selective preservation of semantically vital tokens, dynamic computational allocation at uncertain decision boundaries, multi-path exploration at ambiguities, and architectures aligned with natural semantic domains. This marks a fundamental shift from raw scaling to strategic reasoning, promising breakthrough performance without proportionate computational scaling and offering a more nuanced understanding that supersedes the exponential decay hypothesis, thereby opening pathways toward substantially more powerful and efficient language systems.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Beyond Exponential Decay: Rethinking Error Accumulation in Large Language Models."

**Summary:**

The paper challenges the prevailing assumption that Large Language Model (LLM) reliability degrades exponentially with sequence length, based on the idea that errors are independent across tokens. The authors argue that this view is overly simplistic and doesn't align with the observed capabilities of LLMs to generate coherent long texts. Instead, they propose that LLM errors are concentrated at sparse "key tokens" (5-10% of total tokens) representing critical decision junctions. They introduce a new reliability formula that accounts for the heterogeneous nature of token importance, arguing that long-context performance depends on accurately navigating these crucial semantic decision points rather than uniform token-level accuracy. The paper integrates insights from attention patterns, embedding geometry, and multi-path reasoning to support this framework. They suggest strategies centered on selective preservation of semantically vital tokens, dynamic computational allocation, multi-path exploration, and architectures aligned with natural semantic domains, promising improved performance without proportionate computational scaling. The authors back their theoretical framework with empirical evidence.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a compelling alternative framework for understanding error accumulation in LLMs, moving beyond the overly simplistic exponential decay model. The core idea that errors are not uniformly distributed but rather concentrated at key decision points is insightful and well-supported by existing research.

**Novelty:** The novelty lies in synthesizing several emerging research streams (attention sparsity, stratified manifolds, self-consistency) into a unified framework. The paper doesn't necessarily present groundbreaking new empirical findings but rather provides a coherent theoretical explanation for observed LLM behavior. While some individual concepts (e.g., key tokens, stratified manifolds) have been explored separately, the paper's unique contribution is in integrating them to create a holistic picture of error dynamics. The refinement of the error formula that incorporates these factors is novel, if not particularly profound mathematically. This synthesis leads to new practical implications, making the work non-obvious.

**Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from its potential to shift the focus of LLM research. By highlighting the importance of key decision points, the authors suggest that improvements in long-context performance can be achieved through targeted strategies rather than simply scaling parameters. This has significant implications for resource allocation, model architecture design, and prompt engineering. The paper could influence the development of more efficient and reliable LLMs. The paper has some influence within the artificial intelligence community because it provides helpful insights and a framework to guide future research.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear and Coherent Argument:** The paper presents a well-structured and logical argument, making it easy to follow.
*   **Strong Empirical Support:** The framework is supported by a substantial body of empirical evidence from recent studies.
*   **Practical Implications:** The paper outlines concrete strategies for improving LLM performance based on its theoretical framework.
*   **Synthesis of Existing Research:** The paper effectively synthesizes several emerging research streams into a unified perspective.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Lack of New Empirical Data:** The paper primarily relies on existing empirical evidence, lacking novel experiments to directly validate its framework. This could be seen as a significant limitation.
*   **Qualitative Arguments:** While the framework is supported by data, certain elements, particularly concerning the precise nature of the scaling laws, remain qualitative and somewhat speculative.
*   **Limited Scope:** While the paper offers a more nuanced view of errors, it doesn’t address all aspects of LLM failure (e.g., factual errors due to limited knowledge).

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a well-reasoned and compelling alternative to the simplistic exponential decay model of LLM reliability. It synthesizes existing research into a coherent framework with practical implications for improving LLM performance. While the paper lacks novel empirical data and some aspects of the framework remain qualitative, its contribution in reframing our understanding of error accumulation in LLMs is significant and could influence future research directions. The novelty is reasonable because it takes the separate ideas and combines them to present a new framework. It provides helpful and insightful information.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Fine-Tune an SLM or Prompt an LLM? The Case of Generating Low-Code Workflows
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24189v1)
- **Authors**: Orlando Marquez Ayala, Patrice Bechard, Emily Chen, Maggie Baird, Jingfei Chen
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) such as GPT-4o can handle a wide range of complex tasks with the right prompt. As per token costs are reduced, the advantages of fine-tuning Small Language Models (SLMs) for real-world applications -- faster inference, lower costs -- may no longer be clear. In this work, we present evidence that, for domain-specific tasks that require structured outputs, SLMs still have a quality advantage. We compare fine-tuning an SLM against prompting LLMs on the task of generating low-code workflows in JSON form. We observe that while a good prompt can yield reasonable results, fine-tuning improves quality by 10% on average. We also perform systematic error analysis to reveal model limitations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper investigates whether fine-tuning a Small Language Model (SLM) offers advantages over prompting Large Language Models (LLMs) for a domain-specific task: generating low-code workflows in JSON format. The authors compare fine-tuning an SLM (Mistral-Nemo-12B-Base) against prompting various LLMs (GPT-40, Gemini-2.0 Flash, and others). They decompose the workflow generation task into two sub-tasks: generating the workflow outline and populating the step inputs. They find that fine-tuning the SLM improves quality by about 10% on average compared to prompting LLMs, as measured by their custom Flow Similarity (FlowSim) metric. The authors also conduct a systematic error analysis to identify model limitations.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's contribution lies in providing empirical evidence for the continued relevance of SLM fine-tuning in a specific, structured output generation task.  While the core idea of fine-tuning vs. prompting is not entirely new, the paper provides valuable insights in the context of low-code workflow generation. The creation and application of the FlowSim metric, while perhaps not groundbreaking, is tailored to the task at hand and contributes to the rigor of the comparison. The decomposition into subtasks and the detailed error analysis add further value. However, the methodology (RAG+fine-tuning) is somewhat incremental on established approaches.

*   **Significance:** Low-code/no-code platforms are gaining traction, and the automatic generation of workflows is a crucial aspect of their usability. Demonstrating that fine-tuned SLMs can outperform prompted LLMs in generating these structured workflows has practical implications. The error analysis approach is also significant, since it can be reused in other tasks. The analysis highlights the strengths of fine-tuned SLMs in understanding domain-specific knowledge and generating outputs that adhere to structure rules.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and task decomposition.
    *   Rigorous experimental setup with multiple models and datasets.
    *   Introduction of a custom metric (FlowSim) relevant to the task.
    *   Systematic error analysis that goes beyond simple accuracy metrics.
    *   The use of real-world data and a simulated user interaction scenario.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The novelty is somewhat limited. The core idea is well-established, and the contribution is primarily empirical rather than theoretical. The experimental methodology, while sound, does not break new ground.
    *   The FlowSim metric has limitations (only comparing against one reference workflow, exact string matching for inputs), which the authors acknowledge.
    *   The paper focuses on one specific domain (enterprise workflows) so the generalizability of the results may be limited.
    *   The improvement of 10% while noticeable isn't exceptionally large, and the context of the cost/benefit of managing a separate fine-tuned model is important.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence practitioners in the low-code/no-code space to consider fine-tuning SLMs for generating structured outputs, especially where domain knowledge and structured output are critical. The error analysis approach can be adopted by other researchers working on structured output generation tasks. However, the impact may be limited by the specific nature of the task and the incremental nature of the contributions.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

Given the analysis above, I would assign this paper a score of **7**. It presents solid empirical results showing the value of fine-tuning SLMs for a domain-specific task, supported by a custom metric and thorough error analysis. The paper is well-written and the methodology is sound. While the novelty is not exceptionally high, its clear experimental focus and specific findings contribute meaningfully to the understanding of SLMs vs. LLMs in generating structured output such as low-code workflows.

### CLaSp: In-Context Layer Skip for Self-Speculative Decoding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24196v1)
- **Authors**: Longze Chen, Renke Shan, Huiming Wang, Lu Wang, Ziqiang Liu, Run Luo, Jiawei Wang, Hamid Alinejad-Rokny, Min Yang
- **Abstract**: Speculative decoding (SD) is a promising method for accelerating the decoding process of Large Language Models (LLMs). The efficiency of SD primarily hinges on the consistency between the draft model and the verify model. However, existing drafting approaches typically require additional modules to be trained, which can be challenging to implement and ensure compatibility across various LLMs. In this paper, we propose CLaSp, an in-context layer-skipping strategy for self-speculative decoding. Unlike prior methods, CLaSp does not require additional drafting modules or extra training. Instead, it employs a plug-and-play mechanism by skipping intermediate layers of the verify model to construct a compressed draft model. Specifically, we develop a dynamic programming algorithm that optimizes the layer-skipping process by leveraging the complete hidden states from the last verification stage as an objective. This enables CLaSp to dynamically adjust its layer-skipping strategy after each verification stage, without relying on pre-optimized sets of skipped layers. Experimental results across diverse downstream tasks demonstrate that CLaSp achieves a speedup of 1.3x ~ 1.7x on LLaMA3 series models without altering the original distribution of the generated text.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CLaSp: In-Context Layer Skip for Self-Speculative Decoding."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CLaSp, a novel method for self-speculative decoding that enhances the inference speed of Large Language Models (LLMs). Unlike existing speculative decoding approaches that require training additional modules or pre-optimizing sets of skipped layers, CLaSp dynamically adjusts layer-skipping based on the current context. It skips intermediate layers of the verify model to construct a compressed draft model, using a dynamic programming algorithm to optimize the layer-skipping process. CLaSp leverages the complete hidden states from the last verification stage as an objective to dynamically adjust its layer-skipping strategy. Experimental results on LLaMA3 models demonstrate speedups of 1.3x to 1.7x without altering the original text distribution. The key contributions are: a context-aware dynamic layer-skipping framework, performance optimization strategies for leveraging GPU parallelism, and extensive experimental validation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper tackles a significant problem in the field of LLMs: reducing the inference latency. Speculative decoding is a promising area, and CLaSp offers a new approach to self-speculative decoding by dynamically adjusting layer-skipping in context, eliminating the need for pre-training or extensive optimization.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core idea of in-context, dynamic layer-skipping is a significant contribution. It moves away from static configurations and addresses the limitations of existing methods that struggle with generalization or require extensive pre-optimization. The dynamic programming algorithm used to optimize layer skipping appears to be well-designed and leverages the information already available during speculative decoding.
*   **Significance:** Achieving 1.3x to 1.7x speedups in LLM inference is a substantial improvement, especially considering it doesn't require altering the LLM's parameters or training additional models. This has practical implications for deploying LLMs in real-world applications.
*   **Experimental Validation:** The paper provides a comprehensive evaluation across multiple LLAMA model variants and the Spec-Bench benchmark. Including both greedy and non-greedy settings demonstrates the robustness of the approach. The model size scaling experiments further highlight the potential of CLaSp for larger models.
*   **Clarity:** The paper is well-structured and explains the method clearly. The diagrams are helpful in understanding the pipeline. The ablation studies (especially in the analysis section) highlight the importance of different components of CLaSp.
*   **Practicality:** The CLaSp approach stands out for its *practicality*. It eliminates the need for additional training datasets, fine-tuning, or module replacements, making the approach compatible across various LLMs.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope of Models:** The experiments are primarily focused on the LLaMA family of models. While this is a popular choice, demonstrating the effectiveness of CLaSp on other model architectures (e.g., models with different attention mechanisms or training paradigms) would further strengthen the generalizability claim.  While the approach is *designed* to be portable across various LLMs, the claim is not empirically backed by results on other model families.
*   **Reliance on Parallelism:** The parallel optimization strategy is crucial for minimizing the overhead of the dynamic programming algorithm. While the authors explain this, the performance gains might be less significant in environments with limited GPU resources or specific hardware constraints where the parallelization strategy can't be fully leveraged.
*   **Parameter Sensitivity:**  While the analysis section explores key hyper-parameters, a more in-depth sensitivity analysis would be beneficial.  Understanding how robust the speedup gains are across a broader range of hyperparameter settings and model scales is critical for reproducibility and deployment. The experiments only focused on  LLaMA3-70B for this analysis.
*   **Missing Error Analysis:** While the paper states that the approach does not alter the output distribution, a quantitative comparison (e.g., BLEU score, ROUGE score) with the original model on a held-out dataset would reinforce this claim. Although being lossless is a design target, that target should be empirically validated.
*   **Competition:** Speculative Decoding is an active field of research. While the paper clearly articulates the differences with existing approaches, it should more comprehensively compare against recent advancements that have been made since the literature review in the paper. For instance, more recent (potentially concurrent) approaches that explore dynamic layer skipping approaches in speculative decoding.

**Overall:**

CLaSp offers a valuable contribution to the field of LLM inference acceleration. The dynamic layer-skipping approach is novel and offers practical advantages over existing methods. The experimental results are compelling, although further validation across different models and a more thorough sensitivity analysis would strengthen the claims. The practicality and the improved performance exhibited are very promising for future deployments of LLMs. The weaknesses, while important, don't overshadow the core contributions of the work.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**  The score reflects the paper's significant novelty in addressing a critical problem. The experimental results are promising and demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach, making the paper a valuable contribution. However, the limitations regarding the limited range of models tested and the reliance on parallelization, parameter sensitivity and missing error analysis prevent a higher score. Strong novelty and good execution are somewhat hampered by limited scope of the empirical validation.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Intuitionistic Fuzzy Sets for Large Language Model Data Annotation: A Novel Approach to Side-by-Side Preference Labeling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24199v1)
- **Authors**: Yimin Du
- **Abstract**: The quality of human preference data is crucial for training and evaluating large language models (LLMs), particularly in reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF) and direct preference optimization (DPO) scenarios. Traditional side-by-side (SBS) annotation approaches often struggle with inherent uncertainty, annotator disagreement, and the complexity of preference judgments. This paper introduces a novel framework based on intuitionistic fuzzy sets (IFS) for modeling and aggregating human preferences in LLM data annotation tasks. Our approach captures not only the degree of preference but also the uncertainty and hesitation inherent in human judgment through membership, non-membership, and hesitation degrees. We propose an IFS-based annotation protocol that enables more nuanced preference modeling, develops aggregation methods for handling annotator disagreement, and introduces quality metrics for preference data assessment. Experimental validation on multiple datasets demonstrates that our IFS-based approach significantly improves annotation consistency, reduces annotator fatigue, and produces higher-quality preference data compared to traditional binary and Likert-scale methods. The resulting preference datasets lead to improved model performance in downstream tasks, with 12.3\% improvement in win-rate against baseline models and 15.7\% reduction in annotation time. Our framework provides a principled approach to handling uncertainty in human preference annotation and offers practical benefits for large-scale LLM training.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the paper "Intuitionistic Fuzzy Sets for Large Language Model Data Annotation: A Novel Approach to Side-by-Side Preference Labeling". Here's a summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework for human preference annotation in the context of training large language models (LLMs).  The core idea is to leverage intuitionistic fuzzy sets (IFS) to capture the uncertainty and complexity inherent in human judgments during side-by-side (SBS) evaluation. Instead of forcing annotators to make binary choices or use Likert scales, the proposed method allows them to express their preference using membership, non-membership, and hesitation degrees.  The paper details an IFS-based annotation protocol, aggregation methods for combining annotations from multiple annotators, and quality metrics specific to the IFS framework.  The experimental results demonstrate that the IFS-based approach improves annotation consistency, reduces annotator fatigue, and leads to better downstream model performance compared to traditional methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its application of IFS to the *specific* problem of preference annotation for LLMs. While IFS is a well-established concept, and fuzzy methods have been used in NLP before (as the paper acknowledges), its application to this context, with a specially designed annotation protocol, aggregation methods, and quality metrics, does offer a novel contribution. The claim of novelty, however, is slightly weakened by the fact that existing research has already begun to acknowledge the need to go beyond binary/Likert scales for preference annotation, although with simpler "tie" mechanisms.

**Significance:** The significance stems from the critical role that high-quality preference data plays in training modern LLMs using techniques like RLHF and DPO.  By improving annotation quality and reducing annotator fatigue, this work has the potential to make LLM training more efficient and effective. The improvement in downstream task performance (12.3% in win rate) provides strong evidence of its significance.  Moreover, the framework provides a more nuanced and expressive means of collecting human preferences, potentially leading to better aligned and safer AI systems.  The paper's emphasis on practical implementation, with open-source annotation tools and aggregation algorithms, further increases its potential impact.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of traditional preference annotation methods.
*   **Well-Motivated Approach:** The use of IFS is well-motivated by the need to capture uncertainty in human judgment.
*   **Comprehensive Framework:** The paper provides a complete framework, from annotation protocol to aggregation methods and quality metrics.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results consistently demonstrate the benefits of the IFS-based approach across multiple datasets and tasks.
*   **Practical Implementation:** The availability of annotation tools and aggregation algorithms enhances the practical value of the research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:**  While the application of IFS to LLM preference annotation is novel, the core concept of IFS itself is not new. Existing simple solutions for uncertainty handling such as "tie options" are not throughly compared against.
*   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the framework is mathematically sound, a deeper theoretical analysis of the properties of IFS in the context of preference annotation could strengthen the paper.  For example, discussing the relationship between hesitation degree and annotation quality in more detail.
*   **Complexity Trade-off:** While the paper argues that the IFS approach reduces cognitive load, there might be edge cases or annotators who find the three-dimensional judgment space more complex than binary choices. A better discussion of this trade-off is required.
*   **Limited Scope of Datasets:** The datasets are good, but it would be beneficial to see the approach evaluated on an even wider range of tasks and domains, particularly those with more subjective evaluation criteria.
*  **Weighting factors discussion:** The paper assumes weighting factors. In real world implementations the weighting factors should be learnt. How the weights can be learnt is not well explained.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed application of intuitionistic fuzzy sets to a critical problem in the field of LLM training. The empirical results are strong, and the practical implementation enhances the potential impact of the research. However, the incremental nature of the novelty, some limitations in the theoretical analysis, and the scope of datasets used hold it back from receiving a higher score.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Aligning Protein Conformation Ensemble Generation with Physical Feedback
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24203v1)
- **Authors**: Jiarui Lu, Xiaoyin Chen, Stephen Zhewen Lu, Aurélie Lozano, Vijil Chenthamarakshan, Payel Das, Jian Tang
- **Abstract**: Protein dynamics play a crucial role in protein biological functions and properties, and their traditional study typically relies on time-consuming molecular dynamics (MD) simulations conducted in silico. Recent advances in generative modeling, particularly denoising diffusion models, have enabled efficient accurate protein structure prediction and conformation sampling by learning distributions over crystallographic structures. However, effectively integrating physical supervision into these data-driven approaches remains challenging, as standard energy-based objectives often lead to intractable optimization. In this paper, we introduce Energy-based Alignment (EBA), a method that aligns generative models with feedback from physical models, efficiently calibrating them to appropriately balance conformational states based on their energy differences. Experimental results on the MD ensemble benchmark demonstrate that EBA achieves state-of-the-art performance in generating high-quality protein ensembles. By improving the physical plausibility of generated structures, our approach enhances model predictions and holds promise for applications in structural biology and drug discovery.
- **Summary**: Here is a concise summary of the research paper and a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Energy-based Alignment (EBA), a novel framework for improving protein conformation ensemble generation using diffusion models. EBA integrates physical feedback by aligning the generative model with physical energy landscapes derived from force fields. This is achieved via a scalable learning objective that balances conformational states based on their energy differences. The method is validated on the ATLAS MD ensemble dataset, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance in generating high-quality, physically plausible protein ensembles. The paper highlights the potential of EBA for enhancing model predictions and its applicability in structural biology and drug discovery.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of protein structure prediction and molecular dynamics by addressing the challenge of incorporating physical constraints into data-driven generative models.  The core idea of EBA, aligning a diffusion model's generated conformations with the underlying physical energy landscape, is a logical and potentially impactful step beyond purely data-driven approaches.

* **Novelty:** The combination of a denoising diffusion model with energy-based alignment for protein ensemble generation is novel.  While diffusion models have been applied to protein structure prediction before, the explicit integration of physical feedback through a scalable learning objective sets this work apart. The connection to Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) provides further theoretical grounding and places the work within a broader context of alignment methods. The use of a stochastic mini-batch approximation to tackle the intractable partition function is a practical and effective contribution.

* **Significance:**  The ability to generate physically plausible protein ensembles has broad implications.  More accurate ensembles can lead to better understanding of protein dynamics, improved drug design by targeting multiple conformations, and enhanced structural biology research. The experimental results demonstrate a clear improvement over existing methods on the ATLAS benchmark. The fact that EBA improves ensemble observables, specifically the Jaccard similarity of weak and transient contacts and the Spearman correlation of the exposed MI matrix, suggests that EBA can better capture the long-range dynamics crucial for protein function.

* **Strengths:**
    *   The EBA framework is well-motivated and clearly explained.
    *   The paper demonstrates a strong understanding of both generative modeling and molecular dynamics.
    *   The experimental results are compelling and show a clear improvement over existing methods.
    *   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of different components.
    *   The connection between EBA and DPO is a valuable theoretical contribution.
    *   The use of AlphaFold3 architecture offers a high-quality base for improvement
    *   The method has been designed with scaling in mind
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on pre-computed energy labels limits the method's adaptivity during training. An on-policy approach could further improve the results, although this would increase computational cost and complexity.
    *   The accuracy of the generated structure rely upon the employed force field which can impact the overall performance.
    *   The evaluation is focused on the ATLAS dataset, which is based on MD simulations. While these simulations are valuable, it would be useful to validate EBA on experimental data (e.g., NMR ensembles) to further assess its physical realism.

* **Potential Impact:** This work has the potential to significantly influence the field of protein structure prediction and molecular dynamics. By providing a practical and effective way to incorporate physical constraints into generative models, EBA can enable more accurate and reliable predictions of protein ensembles. This, in turn, could lead to advances in drug discovery, protein engineering, and basic research on protein function.

Overall, the paper presents a well-developed and thoroughly evaluated method with clear advantages over existing techniques. While there are some limitations, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses, and the work has the potential to make a significant impact.
