### Proactive Guidance of Multi-Turn Conversation in Industrial Search
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24251v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaoyu Li, Xiao Li, Li Gao, Yiding Liu, Xiaoyang Wang, Shuaiqiang Wang, Junfeng Wang, Dawei Yin
- **Abstract**: The evolution of Large Language Models (LLMs) has significantly advanced multi-turn conversation systems, emphasizing the need for proactive guidance to enhance users' interactions. However, these systems face challenges in dynamically adapting to shifts in users' goals and maintaining low latency for real-time interactions. In the Baidu Search AI assistant, an industrial-scale multi-turn search system, we propose a novel two-phase framework to provide proactive guidance. The first phase, Goal-adaptive Supervised Fine-Tuning (G-SFT), employs a goal adaptation agent that dynamically adapts to user goal shifts and provides goal-relevant contextual information. G-SFT also incorporates scalable knowledge transfer to distill insights from LLMs into a lightweight model for real-time interaction. The second phase, Click-oriented Reinforcement Learning (C-RL), adopts a generate-rank paradigm, systematically constructs preference pairs from user click signals, and proactively improves click-through rates through more engaging guidance. This dual-phase architecture achieves complementary objectives: G-SFT ensures accurate goal tracking, while C-RL optimizes interaction quality through click signal-driven reinforcement learning. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our framework achieves 86.10% accuracy in offline evaluation (+23.95% over baseline) and 25.28% CTR in online deployment (149.06% relative improvement), while reducing inference latency by 69.55% through scalable knowledge distillation.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a concise summary of the paper and a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a two-phase framework for proactive guidance in multi-turn conversation systems used in industrial search (Baidu Search AI assistant). The framework addresses challenges of dynamic goal shifts and latency in real-time interactions. The first phase, Goal-adaptive Supervised Fine-Tuning (G-SFT), uses a goal adaptation agent to track and adapt to changes in user goals, providing goal-relevant context and scalable knowledge distillation from larger LLMs into a smaller, faster model. The second phase, Click-oriented Reinforcement Learning (C-RL), utilizes a generate-rank paradigm, employing diverse beam search and click signals to create preference pairs and optimize guidance based on user clicks, aiming to improve click-through rates. Experiments demonstrate improvements in accuracy, click-through rate, and inference latency compared to baseline models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a reasonably novel approach to proactive guidance in multi-turn conversational search systems, particularly in an industrial setting. The combination of G-SFT and C-RL is designed to achieve complementary objectives - goal tracking and interaction quality optimization respectively.

**Strengths:**

*   **Practical Relevance:** The work is presented in the context of a real-world application (Baidu Search AI assistant), increasing its potential for immediate practical impact. The focus on low latency and scalability is crucial for industrial deployment.
*   **Goal Adaptation:** The Goal Adaptation Agent (GAA) within G-SFT is a core contribution addressing the challenge of dynamic user goals, which is a significant problem in multi-turn interactions. The components of GAA - explicit goal analysis, goal-relevant summary, and shift detection signal - are logically sound and contribute to a more effective handling of goal shifts.
*   **Click-oriented Reinforcement Learning:** The use of user click signals to refine the guidance through reinforcement learning is a valuable approach to ensure alignment with user preferences and improve interaction quality, specifically using a generate-rank paradigm to deal with the multi-output requirement.
*   **Knowledge Distillation:** The distillation of knowledge from larger LLMs into smaller models directly addresses the latency issues associated with complex models, making the approach feasible for real-time search applications.
*   **Experimental Results:** The experimental evaluation includes both offline and online metrics, providing a comprehensive assessment of the framework's performance. The reported improvements in accuracy, CTR, and latency are substantial.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of techniques is novel, individual components (e.g., supervised fine-tuning, reinforcement learning, knowledge distillation) are well-established in the field. The paper's primary novelty lies in how these components are integrated and applied to the specific problem of proactive guidance.
*   **Dependency on Click Data:** The C-RL phase relies on user click data, which may not be readily available in all scenarios or for new types of queries. The effectiveness of the system could be limited by the availability and quality of this data.
*   **Limited Novelty of Augment model** The construction of augment model based on Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) seems quite straightforward. More in-depth analysis can be conducted.
*   **Details of the Evaluation Metric Delta GSB:** The paper should illustrate why a "Good vs. Same vs. Bad" evaluation scheme can better access the improvement.
*   **Lack of Detailed Comparison to Recent Work:** While the paper mentions related work, a more detailed comparison to recent approaches in proactive guidance and conversational search is needed to better position the contributions of this work. Specifically, the paper should demonstrate the advantage of using GAA for goal-adaptation compared to directly inputting all of historical dialogues into LLMs.
*   **Generalizability:** The evaluation is primarily based on Baidu Search AI assistant. It would be beneficial to discuss the generalizability of the framework to other search domains or conversational AI applications.
*   **Diversity Evaluation**: While the paper emphasizes the importance of diversity and MMR, it would be more convincing if they included metrics directly measuring the "semantic diversity" of the generated guidance (e.g., using pre-trained embeddings and calculating the cosine distances among the guidance).

**Significance:**

The paper contributes to the growing body of work on proactive guidance and conversational search. The proposed framework has the potential to improve user experience, engagement, and satisfaction in search AI assistants. The focus on practical concerns such as latency and scalability makes the work particularly relevant for industrial applications. The paper also introduces a robust evaluation framework with extensive experiments on industrial datasets.

**Justification of Score:**

Overall, the paper presents a solid contribution to the field, demonstrating a practically relevant and well-engineered solution to a challenging problem. The combination of established techniques with the novel GAA module and click-driven reinforcement learning strategy shows the strengths of the work. However, the incremental novelty, dependency on click data, and lack of detailed comparison to existing work slightly diminish the overall impact.

### Interactive Video Generation via Domain Adaptation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24253v1)
- **Authors**: Ishaan Rawal, Suryansh Kumar
- **Abstract**: Text-conditioned diffusion models have emerged as powerful tools for high-quality video generation. However, enabling Interactive Video Generation (IVG), where users control motion elements such as object trajectory, remains challenging. Recent training-free approaches introduce attention masking to guide trajectory, but this often degrades perceptual quality. We identify two key failure modes in these methods, both of which we interpret as domain shift problems, and propose solutions inspired by domain adaptation. First, we attribute the perceptual degradation to internal covariate shift induced by attention masking, as pretrained models are not trained to handle masked attention. To address this, we propose mask normalization, a pre-normalization layer designed to mitigate this shift via distribution matching. Second, we address initialization gap, where the randomly sampled initial noise does not align with IVG conditioning, by introducing a temporal intrinsic diffusion prior that enforces spatio-temporal consistency at each denoising step. Extensive qualitative and quantitative evaluations demonstrate that mask normalization and temporal intrinsic denoising improve both perceptual quality and trajectory control over the existing state-of-the-art IVG techniques.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of interactive video generation (IVG), specifically focusing on trajectory control (guiding the motion of objects within a video based on user-specified bounding boxes). The authors argue that current training-free IVG methods using attention masking often sacrifice perceptual quality for trajectory accuracy. They identify two primary issues: (1) internal covariate shift caused by applying attention masks to pre-trained models not trained on masked data, and (2) an "initialization gap" where random initial noise doesn't align with the conditioning information.  They propose two solutions inspired by domain adaptation techniques: (1) *mask normalization*, a pre-normalization layer that aligns feature distributions between masked and unmasked attention outputs, and (2) *temporal intrinsic denoising*, which leverages the diffusion prior to enforce spatio-temporal consistency during the denoising process, guided by classifier-free gradients from a bounding box temporal prior.  The paper claims that their method improves both perceptual quality and trajectory control compared to existing state-of-the-art IVG techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Mask Normalization:** The idea of using a pre-normalization layer to address internal covariate shift caused by attention masking is reasonably novel in the context of video diffusion models for IVG. Drawing inspiration from style transfer (where feature distribution matching is common) is a sound analogy.
    *   **Temporal Intrinsic Denoising:** The concept of using the diffusion model's prior to refine the initial noise and guide the process with a temporal prior derived from bounding boxes also adds to novelty. This is inspired by Deep Image Prior (DIP) for image denoising and extends it to the temporal domain for video.
*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper addresses a practical and important problem: enabling more fine-grained control over video generation without sacrificing quality.
    *   Identifying the internal covariate shift and initialization gap as key failure modes is a valuable contribution to understanding the limitations of current methods.
    *   The proposed solutions are relatively lightweight and do not require retraining, which is a significant advantage for usability.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper provides a clear problem statement and identifies specific limitations of existing methods.
    *   The proposed solutions are well-motivated and explained, drawing connections to established concepts in domain adaptation and image denoising.
    *   The experimental evaluation includes both qualitative and quantitative results, comparing against relevant baselines (Peekaboo and Trailblazer).
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each proposed component.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The quantitative evaluation, while present, could be more rigorous. For example, relying heavily on metrics used by previous papers (like those used by Peekaboo) can be limiting if those metrics are not ideal. Metrics like JeDi Score have caveats which the authors themselves mention.
    *   The reliance on the Zeroscope model means the generalizability of the approach to other video diffusion architectures is not explicitly demonstrated.
    *   The paper focuses on single-object, static-camera scenarios, which limits the broader applicability of the method. The authors acknowledge this, but the limitations need to be considered.
    *   The paper claims SOTA performance but doesn't necessarily demonstrate a *substantial* improvement over existing methods. The quantitative results show marginal gains in some cases.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper's analysis of the internal covariate shift and initialization gap could influence future research on training-free IVG methods.
    *   The proposed mask normalization and temporal intrinsic denoising techniques could be adopted or adapted by other researchers working in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7**.

*   The paper addresses a relevant problem in the field of video generation.
*   The identification of the internal covariate shift and initialization gap is valuable.
*   The proposed techniques are reasonably novel and well-motivated.
*   However, the experimental validation could be stronger, and the scope of the method is limited to single-object, static-camera scenarios.  The improvements are incremental rather than groundbreaking.

### Effects of Theory of Mind and Prosocial Beliefs on Steering Human-Aligned Behaviors of LLMs in Ultimatum Games
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24255v1)
- **Authors**: Neemesh Yadav, Palakorn Achananuparp, Jing Jiang, Ee-Peng Lim
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown potential in simulating human behaviors and performing theory-of-mind (ToM) reasoning, a crucial skill for complex social interactions. In this study, we investigate the role of ToM reasoning in aligning agentic behaviors with human norms in negotiation tasks, using the ultimatum game as a controlled environment. We initialized LLM agents with different prosocial beliefs (including Greedy, Fair, and Selfless) and reasoning methods like chain-of-thought (CoT) and varying ToM levels, and examined their decision-making processes across diverse LLMs, including reasoning models like o3-mini and DeepSeek-R1 Distilled Qwen 32B. Results from 2,700 simulations indicated that ToM reasoning enhances behavior alignment, decision-making consistency, and negotiation outcomes. Consistent with previous findings, reasoning models exhibit limited capability compared to models with ToM reasoning, different roles of the game benefits with different orders of ToM reasoning. Our findings contribute to the understanding of ToM's role in enhancing human-AI interaction and cooperative decision-making. The code used for our experiments can be found at https://github.com/Stealth-py/UltimatumToM.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the impact of Theory of Mind (ToM) reasoning and prosocial beliefs on the behavior of LLM agents in Ultimatum Games.  It uses different reasoning methods (Chain-of-Thought (CoT), zero-order ToM, first-order ToM, combined ToM) and initializes agents with varying prosocial beliefs (Greedy, Fair, Selfless). The authors run simulations, analyze the agents' decision-making process via metrics like acceptance rate, average turns, and deviation from human norms, and evaluate different LLMs (including reasoning models 03-mini and DeepSeek-R1 Distilled Qwen 32B). The key findings include: fair-fair belief combinations achieve the highest alignment with human norms, ToM reasoning enhances behavior alignment, and different ToM levels are more effective depending on the role.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper provides a good exploration and analysis that aims to contribute to the field of human-AI interaction, specifically with respect to cooperative decision-making by looking at ToM reasonings. Prior work has looked at similar domains, this paper adds additional perspectives and an exploration of ToM. It doesn't, however, present any radically new methodological approaches.

*   **Significance:** The work tackles an important aspect of LLM research: aligning agentic behavior with human values and social norms. Understanding how to incorporate ToM reasoning into LLMs to achieve human-aligned behavior is crucial. The findings contribute to the understanding of ToM's role in enhancing human-AI interaction and cooperative decision-making.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The experimental setup is well-defined, utilizing the Ultimatum Game as a controlled environment.
    *   The inclusion of different LLMs and prosocial beliefs provides a relatively comprehensive comparison.
    *   The use of deviation scores from human norms is a reasonable way to measure behavioral alignment.
    *   The paper identifies specific benefits of different ToM levels based on role (Proposer vs. Responder).
    *   Thorough sensitivity analysis

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper acknowledges the limitations of using simulations and the difficulty of generalizing findings to other scenarios.
    *   The reliance on predefined actions for agents limits the complexity of decision-making.
    *   The assumption of rationality and fixed prosocial beliefs neglects human emotional influence and dynamics.
    *   The authors do not validate ToM performance of LLMs which is a key aspect, though they state that isn't what they want to look at
    *   Human expectations defined are static.
    *   The specific prompt choices have a significant impact on results, and although the prompts are in the appendix, a more thorough discussion of the prompt engineering process and potential sensitivities is warranted.
    *   The significance of the reported OLS regression results could be strengthened with additional validation.

*   **Impact:** The study's findings could inform the development of more human-aligned AI agents, especially in negotiation and cooperative scenarios. It highlights the potential of ToM reasoning and suggests strategies for improving behavior alignment across LLMs.

*   **Overall Assessment:** The paper presents a worthwhile exploration with a clear experimental design. While the novelty is incremental and the experimental design comes with several limitations, the results are thorough and potentially useful.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:** The paper makes a valuable, albeit incremental, contribution. It builds upon existing work on agent-based simulations and LLMs by exploring the effects of ToM and prosocial beliefs in a controlled setting. It contributes to an important field, but more rigorous evaluations and the incorporation of real-world data or human experiments could potentially push the paper to higher impact and novelty levels.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### FABLE: A Novel Data-Flow Analysis Benchmark on Procedural Text for Large Language Model Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24258v1)
- **Authors**: Vishal Pallagani, Nitin Gupta, John Aydin, Biplav Srivastava
- **Abstract**: Understanding how data moves, transforms, and persists, known as data flow, is fundamental to reasoning in procedural tasks. Despite their fluency in natural and programming languages, large language models (LLMs), although increasingly being applied to decisions with procedural tasks, have not been systematically evaluated for their ability to perform data-flow reasoning. We introduce FABLE, an extensible benchmark designed to assess LLMs' understanding of data flow using structured, procedural text. FABLE adapts eight classical data-flow analyses from software engineering: reaching definitions, very busy expressions, available expressions, live variable analysis, interval analysis, type-state analysis, taint analysis, and concurrency analysis. These analyses are instantiated across three real-world domains: cooking recipes, travel routes, and automated plans. The benchmark includes 2,400 question-answer pairs, with 100 examples for each domain-analysis combination. We evaluate three types of LLMs: a reasoning-focused model (DeepSeek-R1 8B), a general-purpose model (LLaMA 3.1 8B), and a code-specific model (Granite Code 8B). Each model is tested using majority voting over five sampled completions per prompt. Results show that the reasoning model achieves higher accuracy, but at the cost of over 20 times slower inference compared to the other models. In contrast, the general-purpose and code-specific models perform close to random chance. FABLE provides the first diagnostic benchmark to systematically evaluate data-flow reasoning and offers insights for developing models with stronger procedural understanding.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "FABLE: A Novel Data-Flow Analysis Benchmark on Procedural Text for Large Language Model Evaluation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FABLE, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the data-flow reasoning abilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) when processing procedural text. FABLE adapts eight classical data-flow analysis techniques from software engineering (reaching definitions, very busy expressions, etc.) and applies them to three real-world domains: cooking recipes, travel routes, and automated plans. The benchmark comprises 2,400 question-answer pairs. The authors evaluate the performance of a reasoning-focused model (deepseek-r1:8b), a general-purpose model (llama3.1:8b), and a code-specific model (granite-code:8b) on this benchmark. The results show that the reasoning-focused model achieves higher accuracy, but at a substantial computational cost, while the other models perform poorly. FABLE aims to provide a diagnostic tool for developing LLMs with improved procedural understanding.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty of this paper lies in the adaptation of data-flow analysis techniques from software engineering to the evaluation of LLMs' ability to understand procedural text. While benchmarks for procedural reasoning exist, they often focus on isolated aspects like entity state tracking or causal relationships. FABLE attempts a more comprehensive assessment by encompassing a broader range of data-flow analysis concepts. The combination of these techniques and the application domains is novel.
*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the increasing use of LLMs in applications that require reasoning about procedural tasks (e.g., robotics, process automation). Existing LLMs, despite their fluency, often struggle with understanding the intricate data dependencies within procedures. FABLE addresses this gap by providing a targeted benchmark that can help identify the weaknesses of LLMs in this area and drive the development of more robust and reliable models. The benchmark’s ability to diagnose specific shortcomings makes it a valuable contribution.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The benchmark incorporates a diverse set of data-flow analyses, providing a comprehensive assessment of LLMs' procedural reasoning capabilities.
    *   **Real-World Domains:** The use of cooking recipes, travel routes, and automated plans makes the benchmark relevant to practical applications.
    *   **Diagnostic Capabilities:** FABLE's design allows for identifying specific weaknesses in LLMs' understanding of procedural text.
    *   **Clear Evaluation Methodology:** The paper presents a well-defined evaluation methodology, including baseline models, evaluation metrics, and statistical analysis.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Model Types:** The evaluation only considers three model types. Expanding the benchmark to include more diverse LLM architectures (e.g., larger models, models trained with specific procedural reasoning objectives) would enhance the robustness of the findings.
    *   **Simplified Questions:** The reliance on binary yes/no questions, while simplifying evaluation, might not fully capture the nuances of LLMs' reasoning processes.  More complex question formats (e.g., generating justifications, explaining reasoning steps) could provide deeper insights.
    *   **Template-Based Approach:** The use of templates for generating questions, while ensuring consistency, might limit the diversity and complexity of the benchmark.
    *   **Potential Domain Bias:** Despite choosing three different domains, there's still a possibility of introducing bias through the selection of the specific recipes, travel routes, and plans used.
*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field by stimulating research on improving the procedural reasoning capabilities of LLMs. FABLE can serve as a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners alike.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

While the paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of LLM evaluation, the weaknesses related to limited model types, potentially over-simplified question formats, and potential domain bias prevent it from achieving a higher score. The adaptation of data-flow analysis techniques is innovative and addresses a crucial gap in existing benchmarks. The benchmark's diagnostic capabilities are also a significant strength. However, the limitations indicate areas for future improvement that could further enhance the benchmark's impact and utility. A score of 7 reflects the paper's solid contribution, recognizing its novelty and potential impact, while acknowledging the need for further refinement.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Generative AI for Urban Design: A Stepwise Approach Integrating Human Expertise with Multimodal Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24260v1)
- **Authors**: Mingyi He, Yuebing Liang, Shenhao Wang, Yunhan Zheng, Qingyi Wang, Dingyi Zhuang, Li Tian, Jinhua Zhao
- **Abstract**: Urban design is a multifaceted process that demands careful consideration of site-specific constraints and collaboration among diverse professionals and stakeholders. The advent of generative artificial intelligence (GenAI) offers transformative potential by improving the efficiency of design generation and facilitating the communication of design ideas. However, most existing approaches are not well integrated with human design workflows. They often follow end-to-end pipelines with limited control, overlooking the iterative nature of real-world design. This study proposes a stepwise generative urban design framework that integrates multimodal diffusion models with human expertise to enable more adaptive and controllable design processes. Instead of generating design outcomes in a single end-to-end process, the framework divides the process into three key stages aligned with established urban design workflows: (1) road network and land use planning, (2) building layout planning, and (3) detailed planning and rendering. At each stage, multimodal diffusion models generate preliminary designs based on textual prompts and image-based constraints, which can then be reviewed and refined by human designers. We design an evaluation framework to assess the fidelity, compliance, and diversity of the generated designs. Experiments using data from Chicago and New York City demonstrate that our framework outperforms baseline models and end-to-end approaches across all three dimensions. This study underscores the benefits of multimodal diffusion models and stepwise generation in preserving human control and facilitating iterative refinements, laying the groundwork for human-AI interaction in urban design solutions.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces a novel, stepwise generative framework for urban design that combines human expertise with multimodal diffusion models. This framework addresses the limitations of existing GenAI approaches by incorporating both human control and acknowledging the iterative and nonlinear nature of urban design. The design process is broken down into three stages: road network and land use planning, building layout planning, and detailed planning and rendering. At each stage, the process uses a ControlNet diffusion model to generate urban design layouts based on site constraints and planning guidance. The experiments, conducted in Chicago and New York City, demonstrate improvements over GAN-based baselines in terms of visual fidelity, instruction compliance, and visual diversity. The paper also compares the stepwise approach to end-to-end frameworks. It reveals the stepwise framework's superiority in output quality, adherence to instructions, and level of human control. Furthermore, it showcases the framework's capability in generating design alternatives and transferring urban design styles across different cities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of generative AI for urban design. Its novelty lies in the stepwise, human-in-the-loop approach that is aligned with established urban design practices. Existing methods often treat the design as a monolithic end-to-end generation, while the presented framework acknowledges that urban design is a complex, iterative process involving multiple stakeholders. Combining human expertise with AI enhances control, adaptivity, and better integration of GenAI with real-world design workflows.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Framework:** The stepwise approach is a significant departure from existing end-to-end methods, reflecting a deeper understanding of the urban design process.
*   **Human-AI Integration:** The integration of human expertise at each stage allows for control and iterative refinement, addressing a key limitation of current GenAI applications.
*   **Use of Multimodal Diffusion Models:** Using state-of-the-art ControlNet to achieve a desired effect by incorporating both image constraints and textual prompts is an important application of existing methodologies.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The evaluation framework considers visual fidelity, instruction compliance, and design diversity, providing a holistic assessment of the generated designs.
*   **Practical Application:** The paper focuses on generating realistic urban design diagrams rather than abstract images.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Regional Context:** The experiments are limited to two US cities, potentially limiting the framework's applicability to other urban contexts with differing planning regulations and spatial patterns. Generalization beyond grid-based urban landscapes, which are predominant in the US, may be difficult.
*   **Simplification of Complex Factors:** While the stepwise approach is more realistic than end-to-end methods, it still simplifies the complex interplay of socioeconomic, environmental, and political factors that influence urban design. While instruction compliance is met by the algorithm, the human constraints fed to the model might be limited in the scope of such factors.
*   **Qualitative Diversity Evaluation:** The diversity evaluation is primarily qualitative. Developing more quantifiable metrics for design diversity would strengthen the evaluation.
*   **Reliance on Public Datasets:** It's difficult to replicate an algorithm with datasets from open access sources. This can affect the reproducibility.

**Significance:**

The paper is significant because it addresses a critical gap in the existing research on generative AI for urban design. By integrating human expertise and following established urban design practices, the proposed framework makes GenAI more practical and usable for urban planners. The framework enables exploration of multiple design alternatives while ensuring adherence to planning objectives and context-specific requirements. This could greatly enhance the efficiency of design generation and communication, potentially improving urban design processes and public participation.

The paper's focus on iterative refinements with stakeholders at each step is also a very valuable step.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel framework with strong empirical results and practical implications for urban design. While there are limitations related to regional context and simplification of complex factors, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses. The stepwise approach, human-AI integration, and comprehensive evaluation are significant contributions that advance the field of generative AI for urban design. The integration of iterative steps with stakeholders is also a strong contribution.
