- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### TimeHC-RL: Temporal-aware Hierarchical Cognitive Reinforcement Learning for Enhancing LLMs' Social Intelligence
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24500v1)
- **Authors**: Guiyang Hou, Xing Gao, Yuchuan Wu, Xiang Huang, Wenqi Zhang, Zhe Zheng, Yongliang Shen, Jialu Du, Fei Huang, Yongbin Li, Weiming Lu
- **Abstract**: Recently, Large Language Models (LLMs) have made significant progress in IQ-related domains that require careful thinking, such as mathematics and coding. However, enhancing LLMs' cognitive development in social domains, particularly from a post-training perspective, remains underexplored. Recognizing that the social world follows a distinct timeline and requires a richer blend of cognitive modes (from intuitive reactions (System 1) and surface-level thinking to deliberate thinking (System 2)) than mathematics, which primarily relies on System 2 cognition (careful, step-by-step reasoning), we introduce Temporal-aware Hierarchical Cognitive Reinforcement Learning (TimeHC-RL) for enhancing LLMs' social intelligence. In our experiments, we systematically explore improving LLMs' social intelligence and validate the effectiveness of the TimeHC-RL method, through five other post-training paradigms and two test-time intervention paradigms on eight datasets with diverse data patterns. Experimental results reveal the superiority of our proposed TimeHC-RL method compared to the widely adopted System 2 RL method. It gives the 7B backbone model wings, enabling it to rival the performance of advanced models like DeepSeek-R1 and OpenAI-O3. Additionally, the systematic exploration from post-training and test-time interventions perspectives to improve LLMs' social intelligence has uncovered several valuable insights.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "TimeHC-RL: Temporal-aware Hierarchical Cognitive Reinforcement Learning for Enhancing LLMs' Social Intelligence":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new reinforcement learning (RL) method called "TimeHC-RL" designed to improve Large Language Models' (LLMs) performance in social intelligence tasks. The core idea is to enhance LLMs' ability to reason about social situations by incorporating temporal awareness (understanding how events unfold in time) and hierarchical cognitive processing. TimeHC-RL utilizes a novel temporal reward mechanism and a hierarchical cognition framework that allows the LLM to choose between intuitive (System 1), surface-level, and deliberate (System 2) thinking modes. The authors test TimeHC-RL against several baselines using multiple post-training and test-time intervention paradigms on a diverse set of social intelligence datasets. Their results show that TimeHC-RL outperforms other RL methods, including those that rely solely on System 2 reasoning. They demonstrate that the TimeHC-RL enables a 7B model to rival the performance of much larger, more advanced models. The paper also analyzes the impact of different training strategies, highlighting the benefits of RL over supervised fine-tuning and the importance of considering diverse cognitive modes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a decent level of novelty. While the general idea of using RL to improve LLMs' reasoning isn't new, the specific combination of temporal-aware rewards and the hierarchical cognitive framework is a significant contribution. The approach explicitly addresses the limitations of existing methods that primarily focus on System 2 reasoning, acknowledging that social intelligence requires a broader range of cognitive processes. The temporal reward mechanism seems particularly innovative. However, the hierarchical structure of cognitive modes, while intuitively appealing, needs further justification for its specific implementation and potential optimality. The way different cognitive modes are combined in the proposed solution is fairly generic, and thus lacks in-depth engineering novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in addressing a crucial gap in LLM research: enhancing social intelligence. While LLMs have excelled in IQ-related tasks, their social reasoning abilities are still lacking. TimeHC-RL offers a promising approach to improving LLMs' understanding of human interactions and social contexts. The comprehensive experimental evaluation, including comparisons with state-of-the-art models and analysis of different training strategies, provides valuable insights for future research in this area. Moreover, the conclusion that SFT struggles with generalization compared to RL is important for practitioners in the field.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem statement and well-defined research question.
    *   Novel combination of temporal awareness and hierarchical cognitive processing.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation with diverse datasets and baselines.
    *   In-depth analysis of the results and valuable insights into LLM training strategies.
    *   Strong empirical results, demonstrating the effectiveness of TimeHC-RL.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The choice of the specific cognitive modes (System 1, Surface, System 2) could be better justified. Are these the *optimal* modes for social intelligence, or are they simply a convenient starting point? Further exploration of alternative cognitive architectures would strengthen the paper.
    *   While the paper shows improvement in OOD scenarios, the drop in performance compared to In-Domain scenarios is still notable and requires more explanation.
    *   Some of the claims could be made stronger if the experiments included even more diverse and challenging social scenarios.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper is likely to influence future research on LLM social intelligence by providing a concrete and effective method for incorporating temporal reasoning and hierarchical cognitive processing. It also raises important questions about the optimal training strategies and cognitive architectures for social reasoning tasks.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field of LLM social intelligence. The proposed TimeHC-RL method is novel and effective, and the experimental evaluation is thorough and insightful. The core idea of combining temporal awareness with a hierarchical cognitive framework is intuitively appealing. However, the paper could be strengthened by a more in-depth justification of the chosen cognitive modes and further exploration of more challenging social scenarios. The chosen cognitive modes (System 1, Surface, System 2) have a flavor of ad-hoc selection, which are not rigorously justified. While the results are promising, some aspects of novelty are limited by the generic ways cognitive modes are combined. Overall, the paper represents a significant step forward in enhancing LLMs' social reasoning abilities.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### UniGeo: Taming Video Diffusion for Unified Consistent Geometry Estimation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24521v1)
- **Authors**: Yang-Tian Sun, Xin Yu, Zehuan Huang, Yi-Hua Huang, Yuan-Chen Guo, Ziyi Yang, Yan-Pei Cao, Xiaojuan Qi
- **Abstract**: Recently, methods leveraging diffusion model priors to assist monocular geometric estimation (e.g., depth and normal) have gained significant attention due to their strong generalization ability. However, most existing works focus on estimating geometric properties within the camera coordinate system of individual video frames, neglecting the inherent ability of diffusion models to determine inter-frame correspondence. In this work, we demonstrate that, through appropriate design and fine-tuning, the intrinsic consistency of video generation models can be effectively harnessed for consistent geometric estimation. Specifically, we 1) select geometric attributes in the global coordinate system that share the same correspondence with video frames as the prediction targets, 2) introduce a novel and efficient conditioning method by reusing positional encodings, and 3) enhance performance through joint training on multiple geometric attributes that share the same correspondence. Our results achieve superior performance in predicting global geometric attributes in videos and can be directly applied to reconstruction tasks. Even when trained solely on static video data, our approach exhibits the potential to generalize to dynamic video scenes.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "UniGeo: Taming Video Diffusion for Unified Consistent Geometry Estimation" introduces a novel framework, UniGeo, that leverages pre-trained video diffusion models for estimating consistent geometric properties (e.g., surface normals, position) from either multi-view images or video sequences. Instead of estimating geometry within individual camera coordinate systems, UniGeo predicts geometric attributes in a unified global coordinate frame. The key ideas include: (1) targeting global geometric attributes aligned with video frame correspondences, (2) a novel conditioning method using shared positional encodings to effectively use video diffusion models, and (3) joint training on multiple geometric attributes. Experiments show UniGeo achieves superior performance in global geometric attribute prediction and can be directly applied to reconstruction tasks, even generalizing to dynamic video scenes despite being trained on static data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper presents several novel aspects. The most significant is the unified approach to geometry estimation using video diffusion models in a *global* coordinate system. This addresses the inconsistency issues inherent in frame-wise estimations done in local camera coordinates, which is a significant leap forward. The use of shared positional encodings (SPE) is also a novel and efficient conditioning method that sidesteps architectural modifications of the pre-trained diffusion model, allowing better prior leveraging. Furthermore, the multi-attribute joint training approach provides a performance boost and simplifies the framework.

**Significance:**

The potential impact of this work on the field of 3D geometry estimation and reconstruction is significant. By achieving consistent geometry estimation across video frames, UniGeo paves the way for improved downstream tasks like 3D scene understanding, VR/AR, and robotics. The fact that the method can generalize to dynamic scenes, even when trained on static data, highlights the robustness and generalizability of the approach. This addresses the critical challenge of learning from large scale datasets that are primarily static scenes.  The unified framework predicting multiple geometric properties with a single model also contributes to efficiency and simplifies the deployment process.

**Strengths:**

*   **Global Coordinate System:**  A key contribution, addressing a fundamental limitation of previous frame-wise methods.
*   **Shared Positional Encoding:** An elegant conditioning mechanism that minimizes architectural changes and enables effective prior leveraging.
*   **Multi-Attribute Joint Training:** Efficiently integrates different geometric properties, improving overall performance.
*   **Strong Results:** Demonstrates superior performance on various datasets and reconstruction tasks.
*   **Generalizability:** Shows robust performance in dynamic scenes, even with training on static data.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Synthetic Data Dependency:** The method is trained exclusively on synthetic data. While it shows promise for generalization, real-world performance needs further investigation.
*   **Limited Segment Lengths:**  The paper acknowledges the limitation of processing only short video segments due to storage constraints.  Addressing this is critical for wider applicability, and no concrete solutions are proposed.
*   **Resolution Limits:** The relatively low training resolution might lead to blurry artifacts. Model distillation is suggested as a possible solution, but hasn’t been tested.
*   **Incremental Improvements:** While the individual components (SPE, Multi-attribute training) are novel, the framework as a whole combines a number of known techniques (finetuning diffusion, joint training).

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes several important contributions to the field of 3D geometry estimation, addressing inconsistencies in video data through an innovative application of video diffusion models. The use of a global coordinate system and shared positional encodings are key strengths. However, the dependence on synthetic training data, limitations on segment length, and lack of quantitative results on real-world dynamic data slightly detract from its overall impact. While the framework combines several existing ideas, the combination is novel and demonstrably effective.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Stress-testing Machine Generated Text Detection: Shifting Language Models Writing Style to Fool Detectors
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24523v1)
- **Authors**: Andrea Pedrotti, Michele Papucci, Cristiano Ciaccio, Alessio Miaschi, Giovanni Puccetti, Felice Dell'Orletta, Andrea Esuli
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in Generative AI and Large Language Models (LLMs) have enabled the creation of highly realistic synthetic content, raising concerns about the potential for malicious use, such as misinformation and manipulation. Moreover, detecting Machine-Generated Text (MGT) remains challenging due to the lack of robust benchmarks that assess generalization to real-world scenarios. In this work, we present a pipeline to test the resilience of state-of-the-art MGT detectors (e.g., Mage, Radar, LLM-DetectAIve) to linguistically informed adversarial attacks. To challenge the detectors, we fine-tune language models using Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) to shift the MGT style toward human-written text (HWT). This exploits the detectors' reliance on stylistic clues, making new generations more challenging to detect. Additionally, we analyze the linguistic shifts induced by the alignment and which features are used by detectors to detect MGT texts. Our results show that detectors can be easily fooled with relatively few examples, resulting in a significant drop in detection performance. This highlights the importance of improving detection methods and making them robust to unseen in-domain texts.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the vulnerability of current machine-generated text (MGT) detectors to adversarial attacks. It proposes a pipeline to fine-tune large language models (LLMs) using Direct Preference Optimization (DPO), guiding them to generate synthetic text stylistically closer to human-written text (HWT). The fine-tuning leverages linguistically-informed datasets and aims to shift the linguistic feature distributions of MGTs towards those of HWTs. The effectiveness of this adversarial approach is evaluated by testing state-of-the-art MGT detectors and human raters on the newly generated text. The results demonstrate that the detectors are relatively easily fooled with few examples, leading to a significant drop in performance, while human performance largely remains unchanged and close to random guessing. The study also analyzes the linguistic features that detectors and human raters exploit, finding limited overlap. The authors release code, models, and data to support future research on robust MGT detection benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear problem statement and motivation:** The paper addresses a relevant and increasingly important issue – the need for robust MGT detection in the face of increasingly sophisticated AI-generated content. The limitations of existing detectors concerning domain adaptation and adversarial attacks are clearly outlined.
    *   **Well-defined methodology:** The proposed pipeline for generating adversarial examples is clearly explained and replicable. The use of DPO for fine-tuning, linguistic feature extraction, and MANOVA testing provides a robust framework.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The study evaluates both machine detectors and human raters, providing a balanced perspective.  The experiments cover two distinct domains (news and scientific writing) and analyze the performance of multiple MGT detection methods. The inclusion of true positive rates (TPR) at low false positive rates (FPR) is valuable.
    *   **Insightful analysis:** The analysis of linguistic features exploited by detectors and human raters provides valuable insights into the shortcomings of current detection methods.
    *   **Replicability:** The release of code, models, and data enhances the reproducibility and impact of the research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scale of Adversarial Attack:** While showing a reduction in effectiveness of various MGT detectors, DPO fine-tuning is performed using just approximately 7k examples. It's uncertain whether current approaches will exhibit a sustained performance reduction when exposed to fine-tuning performed on larger datasets.
    *   **Generalizability to Different Domains:** The study focuses on news articles and scientific abstracts. It's unclear how well the findings generalize to other text domains or modalities (e.g., code, dialogue).
    *   **Complexity of Human Evaluation:** While incorporating human evaluation is a strength, the near-random accuracy suggests the task as presented may be inherently difficult or requires a more tailored approach to ensure reliable data. This reduces the significance of claims about human performance.
    *   **Choice of Detectors:** The detector selection, while reasonable, has scope for improvement. Including a wider variety of cutting-edge zero-shot techniques would be of merit.
    *   **Novelty of Approach:** The application of DPO for adversarial attacks on MGT detectors, while not entirely novel, is a useful adaptation of existing techniques. Similar methods have been applied to related problems, and the paper acknowledges this.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper's main contribution is highlighting a vulnerability in current MGT detection systems and providing a methodology to create adversarial examples, rather than creating a radically new MGT detector. The study is most impactful in terms of demonstrating the shortcomings of stylistic shortcut-based approaches and contributing benchmarks.
    *   The paper shows the ease with which the performance of state-of-the-art MGT detectors can be significantly reduced. This contributes to the broader discussion of AI safety and responsible development.
    *   The release of the adversarial dataset helps advance the field by providing resources for evaluating more robust and generalized MGT detection techniques.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The paper is highly relevant in the current landscape where LLMs are being rapidly deployed. It is likely to influence future research in MGT detection by prompting the development of more robust methods.
    *   The released resources may stimulate further investigation into adversarial attacks on MGT detection and the linguistic features that underpin these vulnerabilities.
    *   It will encourage developers of MGT detection systems to consider adversarial attacks and evaluate their models against more realistic scenarios.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of 7 because the paper makes a solid and relevant contribution to the field of MGT detection. The methodology is well-defined and the evaluation is thorough, demonstrating a significant vulnerability in existing systems. The limitations regarding the generalizability and complexity of human evaluations prevent a higher rating. Overall, this work provides valuable insights and actionable resources for improving MGT detection, prompting further research in a critical direction.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Transformers Are Universally Consistent
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24531v1)
- **Authors**: Sagar Ghosh, Kushal Bose, Swagatam Das
- **Abstract**: Despite their central role in the success of foundational models and large-scale language modeling, the theoretical foundations governing the operation of Transformers remain only partially understood. Contemporary research has largely focused on their representational capacity for language comprehension and their prowess in in-context learning, frequently under idealized assumptions such as linearized attention mechanisms. Initially conceived to model sequence-to-sequence transformations, a fundamental and unresolved question is whether Transformers can robustly perform functional regression over sequences of input tokens. This question assumes heightened importance given the inherently non-Euclidean geometry underlying real-world data distributions. In this work, we establish that Transformers equipped with softmax-based nonlinear attention are uniformly consistent when tasked with executing Ordinary Least Squares (OLS) regression, provided both the inputs and outputs are embedded in hyperbolic space. We derive deterministic upper bounds on the empirical error which, in the asymptotic regime, decay at a provable rate of $\mathcal{O}(t^{-1/2d})$, where $t$ denotes the number of input tokens and $d$ the embedding dimensionality. Notably, our analysis subsumes the Euclidean setting as a special case, recovering analogous convergence guarantees parameterized by the intrinsic dimensionality of the data manifold. These theoretical insights are corroborated through empirical evaluations on real-world datasets involving both continuous and categorical response variables.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the theoretical foundations of Transformers, specifically their ability to perform functional regression over sequences of input tokens, especially in non-Euclidean (hyperbolic) spaces.  It proves that Transformers equipped with softmax attention are uniformly consistent for Ordinary Least Squares (OLS) regression when inputs and outputs are embedded in hyperbolic space. The paper derives a convergence rate for the empirical error and empirically validates these results using real-world datasets with both continuous and categorical response variables.  The authors introduce a novel Hyperbolic Transformer (HyT) architecture built upon the Poincaré ball model to achieve this.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in extending the theoretical analysis of Transformers to hyperbolic space and proving consistency results for this setting. Prior work largely focused on Euclidean space. The introduction of the HyT architecture, specifically designed for hyperbolic data, also contributes to the novelty. While some theoretical results exist for CNNs in hyperbolic spaces, adapting the analysis and proving universal consistency for Transformers in this setting is a non-trivial extension. Additionally, defining hyperbolic attention and feedforward mechanisms suitable for theoretical analysis is novel.

* **Significance:**  The significance stems from two primary aspects: 1)  Real-world data often exhibits non-Euclidean geometry, and this work provides a theoretical justification for using Transformers in such cases. This is increasingly relevant for areas like graph representation learning, hierarchical data modeling, and certain bioinformatics applications where hyperbolic embeddings are beneficial. 2) Establishing consistency is crucial, providing a guarantee that Transformers can learn underlying functions as data increases, which has implications for the reliability of large language models.  However, this is a theoretical paper, with certain restrictions on model architecture for it to work. Real-world utility depends on further work on improving the results to more realistic setups and models.

* **Strengths:**
    *   The theoretical analysis is rigorous, providing explicit convergence rates and conditions.
    *   The introduction of HyT is a valuable contribution, providing a concrete architecture for hyperbolic data.
    *   The empirical results, while preliminary, support the theoretical claims.
    *   The paper addresses an important open question regarding the theoretical guarantees of Transformers.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   The analysis relies on several assumptions, such as specific architectural choices and certain regularity conditions on the data distribution, that might not always hold in practice.  The convergence rate is dependent on the embedding dimensionality *d*, and may not be practical for very high dimensional inputs.
    *   The empirical evaluation is limited.  More extensive experiments on diverse datasets and a more thorough comparison with existing methods would strengthen the results.
    *   The connection between the HyT architecture's design and the specific proof techniques could be made more explicit. Is the architecture uniquely suited for these proofs, or are there other designs that would work as well?

* **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to stimulate further research in applying Transformers to non-Euclidean data and developing more robust theoretical frameworks. It provides a strong foundation for investigating the generalization properties of Transformers in different geometric settings.

* **Overall Assessment:** While the paper makes significant theoretical contributions, its practical impact remains to be seen. The reliance on specific assumptions and the limited empirical evaluation slightly temper the enthusiasm. The paper is well-written, presents original research, and significantly adds to understanding of the theoretical behaviour of Transformers.
Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Beyond Linear Steering: Unified Multi-Attribute Control for Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24535v1)
- **Authors**: Narmeen Oozeer, Luke Marks, Fazl Barez, Amirali Abdullah
- **Abstract**: Controlling multiple behavioral attributes in large language models (LLMs) at inference time is a challenging problem due to interference between attributes and the limitations of linear steering methods, which assume additive behavior in activation space and require per-attribute tuning. We introduce K-Steering, a unified and flexible approach that trains a single non-linear multi-label classifier on hidden activations and computes intervention directions via gradients at inference time. This avoids linearity assumptions, removes the need for storing and tuning separate attribute vectors, and allows dynamic composition of behaviors without retraining. To evaluate our method, we propose two new benchmarks, ToneBank and DebateMix, targeting compositional behavioral control. Empirical results across 3 model families, validated by both activation-based classifiers and LLM-based judges, demonstrate that K-Steering outperforms strong baselines in accurately steering multiple behaviors.
- **Summary**: Here is a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces K-Steering, a novel method for controlling multiple behavioral attributes in large language models (LLMs) simultaneously.  It addresses the limitations of existing linear steering approaches that struggle with attribute interference and require per-attribute tuning.  K-Steering trains a single non-linear multi-label classifier on hidden activations and uses its gradients to compute intervention directions during inference. This eliminates the linearity assumption, removes the need for separate attribute vectors, and enables dynamic composition of behaviors.  The authors introduce two new benchmarks, TONEBANK and DEBATEMIX, to evaluate their method. Empirical results show K-Steering outperforms baselines in steering multiple behaviors accurately, as validated by activation-based classifiers and LLM-based judges.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:*
The novelty of the paper lies primarily in the *unified* approach to multi-attribute steering using a *single* non-linear classifier. Most existing techniques focus on steering individual attributes using linear methods or complex gating mechanisms. The key idea of training a single classifier and using its gradients for steering is a valuable contribution. The introduction of two new datasets, TONEBANK and DEBATEMIX, specifically designed for multi-attribute control is also a notable addition. The semi-automated evaluation protocol using binary search to optimize steering magnitudes is also a practical contribution to the field. The application of Householder reflections for ablation is also novel.

*Significance:*
The significance of this work is substantial because it offers a more scalable and flexible way to control LLMs. Multi-attribute control is a crucial requirement for deploying LLMs in real-world applications where complex behaviors are desired. The improved control and reduced per-attribute tuning efforts offered by K-Steering make it a practical and impactful approach.

*Strengths:*
- The method is technically sound, building upon established gradient-based steering techniques.
- The empirical evaluation is thorough, involving different model families, datasets, and evaluation metrics (classifier and LLM judge).
- The introduction of new datasets addresses a gap in the existing resources for multi-attribute control.
- Ablation studies help quantify the effects of different behaviors.
- The method attempts to preserve LLM fluency and coherence, a common problem with steering.

*Weaknesses:*
- Datasets are custom-built and their generalizability to naturally occurring text is an open question.
- Evaluation mostly focuses on a single-step, single-layer version of K-Steering, while the multi-step version introduces additional computational overhead.
- The improvement of K-Steering over baselines, while present, isn't always overwhelming and can be tied in some cases.
- Some details regarding model selection, baseline implementation, or training may be further refined.

*Potential Influence:*
K-Steering has the potential to significantly influence research on controllable language generation and steering methods. The concept of unified multi-attribute steering could inspire new approaches that move beyond linear interventions and attribute-specific tuning. The provided datasets and evaluation protocols can serve as valuable resources for future work.

*Justification:*
The paper's strengths demonstrate a solid methodological contribution with practical implications. The weaknesses point to avenues for future research and refinements but don't detract from the core value of the work. Overall, K-Steering represents a meaningful advancement in multi-attribute control of LLMs.

### CHIP: Chameleon Hash-based Irreversible Passport for Robust Deep Model Ownership Verification and Active Usage Control
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24536v1)
- **Authors**: Chaohui Xu, Qi Cui, Chip-Hong Chang
- **Abstract**: The pervasion of large-scale Deep Neural Networks (DNNs) and their enormous training costs make their intellectual property (IP) protection of paramount importance. Recently introduced passport-based methods attempt to steer DNN watermarking towards strengthening ownership verification against ambiguity attacks by modulating the affine parameters of normalization layers. Unfortunately, neither watermarking nor passport-based methods provide a holistic protection with robust ownership proof, high fidelity, active usage authorization and user traceability for offline access distributed models and multi-user Machine-Learning as a Service (MLaaS) cloud model. In this paper, we propose a Chameleon Hash-based Irreversible Passport (CHIP) protection framework that utilizes the cryptographic chameleon hash function to achieve all these goals. The collision-resistant property of chameleon hash allows for strong model ownership claim upon IP infringement and liable user traceability, while the trapdoor-collision property enables hashing of multiple user passports and licensee certificates to the same immutable signature to realize active usage control. Using the owner passport as an oracle, multiple user-specific triplets, each contains a passport-aware user model, a user passport, and a licensee certificate can be created for secure offline distribution. The watermarked master model can also be deployed for MLaaS with usage permission verifiable by the provision of any trapdoor-colliding user passports. CHIP is extensively evaluated on four datasets and two architectures to demonstrate its protection versatility and robustness. Our code is released at https://github.com/Dshm212/CHIP.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "CHIP," a Chameleon Hash-based Irreversible Passport framework for deep neural network (DNN) intellectual property (IP) protection. CHIP aims to provide robust ownership verification, active usage control, and user traceability for both offline distributed models and online Machine Learning as a Service (MLaaS) cloud models. It leverages cryptographic chameleon hash functions to generate an immutable signature from the owner's passport and licensor certificate, allowing for strong ownership claims. The trapdoor collision property of the hash enables the creation of user-specific models with unique passports and licensee certificates for active usage control. A skip connection in the passport layer ensures strong dependence between critical affine factors and the passport.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the intelligent integration of chameleon hashes within a passport-based watermarking framework for DNN IP protection. While passport-based methods are not entirely new, the use of chameleon hashes to create both an immutable owner signature and trapdoor-collision based user-specific passports addresses significant limitations in existing approaches. The addition of the skip connection is a clever architectural enhancement that strengthens the link between passports and model behavior.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in the field of DNNs: protecting the valuable IP embedded within these models. Existing methods often fall short in providing a holistic solution that encompasses robust ownership verification, active usage control (both online and offline), and resistance to ambiguity attacks. CHIP's ability to create individualized, actively-controlled user models *without* retraining the master model represents a significant advancement, particularly for scalable deployments. The ability to trace the source of model leakage (traitor tracing) is also a significant advantage.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Holistic Approach:** CHIP addresses multiple aspects of DNN IP protection: ownership verification, active usage control (online/offline), and traitor tracing.
    *   **Scalability:** The trapdoor collision mechanism allows the efficient generation of multiple user models *without* needing to retrain the entire master model. This is a major improvement over other methods.
    *   **Robustness:** The paper provides compelling evidence of CHIP's resilience against ambiguity attacks, even with oracle passports, and against removal attempts through fine-tuning and transfer learning. The skip connection is a key enabler of robustness.
    *   **Versatility:** Demonstrated applicability across image and graph classification tasks increases confidence in general applicability.
    *   **Practical Implementation:**  The inclusion of code and detailed experimental results strengthens the practicality of CHIP.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Complexity:**  Chameleon hash functions introduce cryptographic overhead that may impact training time (although the paper demonstrates manageable overhead). The complexity can be a barrier to entry.
    *   **Assumptions:** The threat model assumes that the owner has complete control over the training pipeline. If the training data or process is compromised, CHIP's effectiveness could be undermined.
    *   **Reliance on a Strong Cryptographic Primitive:** The security of CHIP depends critically on the security of the underlying chameleon hash function. Advances in breaking or compromising these functions could affect CHIP's security.
    *   **Cloud Application limitations:**  While the cloud application is discussed, the evaluation primarily focuses on the more challenging offline deployment. Further empirical validation of CHIP's performance in the cloud is warranted.

*   **Justification of the Score:**

The paper introduces a novel and well-engineered solution to a critical problem with DNNs. CHIP overcomes limitations of existing methods, offering a more comprehensive and practical approach to IP protection. The use of chameleon hashes and the innovative skip connection design are key contributions. While there are some limitations related to the complexity and cryptographic assumptions, the evidence presented in the paper is compelling, demonstrating CHIP's robustness, versatility, and scalability. The paper addresses a critical problem in DNN security and demonstrates a solid step in finding a robust solution, but could use additional cloud testing to ensure full practicality.

- **Score**: 8/10

### Don't Erase, Inform! Detecting and Contextualizing Harmful Language in Cultural Heritage Collections
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24538v1)
- **Authors**: Orfeas Menis Mastromichalakis, Jason Liartis, Kristina Rose, Antoine Isaac, Giorgos Stamou
- **Abstract**: Cultural Heritage (CH) data hold invaluable knowledge, reflecting the history, traditions, and identities of societies, and shaping our understanding of the past and present. However, many CH collections contain outdated or offensive descriptions that reflect historical biases. CH Institutions (CHIs) face significant challenges in curating these data due to the vast scale and complexity of the task. To address this, we develop an AI-powered tool that detects offensive terms in CH metadata and provides contextual insights into their historical background and contemporary perception. We leverage a multilingual vocabulary co-created with marginalized communities, researchers, and CH professionals, along with traditional NLP techniques and Large Language Models (LLMs). Available as a standalone web app and integrated with major CH platforms, the tool has processed over 7.9 million records, contextualizing the contentious terms detected in their metadata. Rather than erasing these terms, our approach seeks to inform, making biases visible and providing actionable insights for creating more inclusive and accessible CH collections.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces an AI-powered tool and associated multilingual vocabulary designed to detect and contextualize harmful language within Cultural Heritage (CH) collections. The key idea is to make historical biases visible rather than simply erasing potentially offensive terms. The vocabulary is co-created with marginalized communities, researchers, and CH professionals and structured as a Knowledge Graph (KG). The tool, accessible via a web application and API, utilizes NLP techniques and LLMs, and has been deployed on platforms like Europeana and MINT, processing millions of records. The paper emphasizes a "human-in-the-loop" approach to balance historical accuracy with ethical concerns, and publishes their manually constructed evaluation dataset for derogatory term detection in CH.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper builds upon existing work in harmful language detection but contributes novel aspects specific to the CH domain. The key novelty lies in:

    *   **Contextualization:**  While harmful language detection is a well-researched area, the focus on contextualizing terms within a *historical* and *cultural heritage* framework, rather than simply classifying them as offensive, is a significant differentiator. This helps move past the “censoring” approach.
    *   **Community Co-creation:** The explicit co-creation of the vocabulary with marginalized communities, researchers, and CH professionals is a strong ethical and methodological contribution, mitigating bias and ensuring cultural sensitivity.
    *   **Knowledge Graph Structure:**  Extending and adapting existing KG schema with bias categories adds structure and scalability for future updates and language expansion.
    *   **Publicly Available Evaluation Dataset:** This resource addresses a critical gap in the field by providing a benchmark specific to derogatory term detection in CH.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in the CH domain. Legacy CH metadata often contains language that reflects historical biases and power structures no longer acceptable today. The work offers practical solutions for CHIs to address this issue. The potential impact includes:

    *   **Enhanced Accessibility:** Making CH collections more inclusive and accessible by mitigating harmful language.
    *   **Improved Understanding:** Providing contextual information allows users to understand the historical context of terms and how they are currently perceived.
    *   **Ethical Curation:** Supporting CH professionals in making informed decisions about how to curate their collections in an ethical manner.
    *   **Transparency:** Increasing transparency by making biases visible rather than masking them.
        *   **Weakness:** The major weakness is the focus on precision, and lack of measuring recall. While it is valid to prioritize avoiding false positives, it prevents any insight into how many derogatory terms are being missed.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Strong emphasis on ethical considerations and community involvement.
    *   Practical tool and resources for CHIs to address harmful language in their collections.
    *   Evaluation focused on minimizing false positives to build trust.
    *   Clear articulation of the challenges specific to the CH domain.
    *   Publicly available vocabulary, ontology, and evaluation dataset.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited detail on the specific prompt engineering used with the LLMs beyond a general description. More details would allow for better reproducibility and further optimization.
    *   Limited generalizability regarding recall.

*   **Potential Influence:** The work is likely to influence how CHIs approach the curation of their collections, encouraging them to move beyond a simple censoring approach and towards a more nuanced and ethical approach that considers context and community perspectives. The public availability of the vocabulary, evaluation dataset, and tool will facilitate further research and development in this area.

**Justification:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field of harmful language detection by focusing on the specific challenges and needs of the cultural heritage domain. The approach of co-creating the vocabulary with marginalized communities and providing contextual information is particularly valuable. The public release of the evaluation dataset is a significant contribution, as it will enable further research and benchmarking in this area. The primary weakness is the limitation regarding measuring and discussing recall.

### Localizing Persona Representations in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24539v1)
- **Authors**: Celia Cintas, Miriam Rateike, Erik Miehling, Elizabeth Daly, Skyler Speakman
- **Abstract**: We present a study on how and where personas -- defined by distinct sets of human characteristics, values, and beliefs -- are encoded in the representation space of large language models (LLMs). Using a range of dimension reduction and pattern recognition methods, we first identify the model layers that show the greatest divergence in encoding these representations. We then analyze the activations within a selected layer to examine how specific personas are encoded relative to others, including their shared and distinct embedding spaces. We find that, across multiple pre-trained decoder-only LLMs, the analyzed personas show large differences in representation space only within the final third of the decoder layers. We observe overlapping activations for specific ethical perspectives -- such as moral nihilism and utilitarianism -- suggesting a degree of polysemy. In contrast, political ideologies like conservatism and liberalism appear to be represented in more distinct regions. These findings help to improve our understanding of how LLMs internally represent information and can inform future efforts in refining the modulation of specific human traits in LLM outputs. Warning: This paper includes potentially offensive sample statements.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates how large language models (LLMs) encode personas – defined by human characteristics, values, and beliefs – within their representation space. The authors use dimensionality reduction and pattern recognition techniques to identify which layers of decoder-only LLMs most strongly encode persona-specific information. They analyze activations within these layers to determine how specific personas are encoded relative to others, focusing on the shared and distinct embedding spaces. Key findings include: (1) persona representations are most prominent in the final third of the decoder layers; (2) ethical perspectives exhibit overlapping activations (polysemy), while political ideologies are represented in more distinct regions of the embedding space; (3) Llama3 exhibits the largest separation across layers and personas. The paper suggests that these findings can inform future efforts to refine the modulation of specific human traits in LLM outputs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Important Research Question:** Understanding how LLMs encode complex human attributes like personas is crucial for responsible AI governance, interpretability, and controllability. The paper addresses a significant and relatively unexplored area.
    *   **Systematic Methodology:** The authors apply a range of established techniques (PCA, Deep Scan, various clustering metrics) in a systematic manner to address their research questions. The methodical approach lends credibility to the findings.
    *   **Comprehensive Persona Set:** The inclusion of personas spanning political ideologies, ethical theories, and personality traits provides a well-rounded basis for analysis.
    *   **Model Comparisons:** Comparing results across multiple LLMs (Llama3, Granite, Mistral) strengthens the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Localization Insights:** The identification of the final third of decoder layers as crucial for persona encoding is a valuable insight.
    *   **Ethical Concerns:** The paper acknowledges potentially offensive sample statements and raises concerns about using LLMs to trigger unintended consequences or bypass safety measures.
    *   **Open-Source Approach:** The paper uses publically available tools and datasets, making the work more reproducible.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty:** The methodology, while systematic, relies on well-established techniques (PCA, Deep Scan). While the *application* of these techniques to persona encoding is novel, the techniques themselves are not.
    *   **Narrow Persona Definitions:**  The personas are defined by brief statements, which risks oversimplifying complex human attributes and potentially introducing bias into the representation learning process. The reliance on model-generated persona statements as a dataset also warrants scrutiny.  The assumptions inherent in the dataset, even if explicitly stated, are a significant limitation.
    *   **Limited Explanation:** The paper identifies *where* personas are encoded but offers limited explanation *why* certain layers or activations are more relevant. This hinders practical application. The explanation focuses primarily on higher-level semantic abstractions.
    *   **Correlational vs. Causal:** The findings are primarily correlational. The paper doesn't establish causal links between specific activations and persona-driven behavior.  Interventionist studies that perturb the identified activations would strengthen the findings.
    *   **Generalizability:** The findings may be specific to the chosen LLMs and the English language. The WEIRD (Western, Educated, Industrialized, Rich, Democratic) perspective of the used datasets limits the generalizability of these findings.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper contributes to the growing body of research on LLM interpretability and control.
    *   The identification of layer-specific encoding patterns provides a foundation for future research on targeted interventions for bias mitigation, personalized model steering, and value alignment.
    *   The observed polysemy in ethical representations raises important questions about the complexities of encoding human values within LLMs.

**Justification of Score:**

Despite its strengths, the paper's limited novelty in methodology and narrow persona definitions are significant drawbacks. The correlational nature of the findings also limits its practical impact. The value lies in providing initial insights into the encoding locations of personas, rather than providing immediately actionable knowledge. While the systematic application of established techniques is useful and well-presented, this contributes less towards a higher novelty score.

Score: 6

The score reflects a solid contribution with useful insights, balanced by methodological limitations and a lack of strong causal claims. The research is important and opens avenues for future work.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Mixpert: Mitigating Multimodal Learning Conflicts with Efficient Mixture-of-Vision-Experts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24541v1)
- **Authors**: Xin He, Xumeng Han, Longhui Wei, Lingxi Xie, Qi Tian
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) require a nuanced interpretation of complex image information, typically leveraging a vision encoder to perceive various visual scenarios. However, relying solely on a single vision encoder to handle diverse task domains proves difficult and inevitably leads to conflicts. Recent work enhances data perception by directly integrating multiple domain-specific vision encoders, yet this structure adds complexity and limits the potential for joint optimization. In this paper, we introduce Mixpert, an efficient mixture-of-vision-experts architecture that inherits the joint learning advantages from a single vision encoder while being restructured into a multi-expert paradigm for task-specific fine-tuning across different visual tasks. Additionally, we design a dynamic routing mechanism that allocates input images to the most suitable visual expert. Mixpert effectively alleviates domain conflicts encountered by a single vision encoder in multi-task learning with minimal additional computational cost, making it more efficient than multiple encoders. Furthermore, Mixpert integrates seamlessly into any MLLM, with experimental results demonstrating substantial performance gains across various tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Mixpert: Mitigating Multimodal Learning Conflicts with Efficient Mixture-of-Vision-Experts" addresses the challenge of domain conflicts in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) that arise from using a single vision encoder for diverse tasks. The authors propose Mixpert, an efficient mixture-of-vision-experts architecture. Mixpert consists of a shared component (shallow layers of the vision encoder) and a multi-expert component, where each expert is specialized in a particular domain. A routing network automatically selects the most suitable expert for an input image. This approach aims to leverage the benefits of joint learning while mitigating domain conflicts.  The authors demonstrate improved performance on various tasks by integrating Mixpert with existing MLLMs like LLaVA-OV, InternVL2, and Qwen2-VL.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using a mixture-of-experts for the vision encoder in MLLMs isn't entirely new (e.g., SPHINX-MOE), but the *specific* way Mixpert is implemented offers some novelty. The key distinctions lie in:

    *   **Manual Expert Domain Definition:** Instead of letting the model learn the expert specialization, the authors manually define the domains based on data types (chart, doc, etc.). This simplifies training and improves interpretability. However, this approach might not be scalable to scenarios where domain knowledge is unavailable.
    *   **Efficient Routing with Shared Component:** The shared component and the routing network keep computational overhead low compared to using multiple full vision encoders. The score-difference routing strategy with a versatile expert for ambiguous inputs is also a valuable addition, addressing a practical problem.
    *   **Focus on Vision Encoder Specialization:**  Prior works often focus on applying MoE structure to LLM part, while Mixpert emphasizes more efficient and specific design on visual encoder.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in:

    *   **Addressing a Real Problem:** The domain conflict in multi-task visual learning is a practical issue that affects MLLM performance. Mixpert provides a tangible solution.
    *   **Improved Performance:** The experimental results consistently show improved performance across different benchmarks and MLLM backbones. This demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed architecture.
    *   **Efficiency:**  The minimal increase in computational cost is a significant advantage, making Mixpert practical for real-world deployment.
    *   **Simplicity and Integration:** Mixpert can be readily integrated into existing MLLMs, facilitating adoption.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-explained architecture and routing mechanism.
    *   Comprehensive experiments with ablation studies.
    *   Strong empirical results demonstrating improved performance and efficiency.
    *   Ablation studies on routing strategy further support the design choices.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Manual Domain Definition:** The reliance on manually defined domains could limit scalability to novel or complex scenarios where such prior knowledge isn't available.  A more adaptive domain assignment could be explored.
    *   **Limited Expert Diversity:** The experts are specialized on a relatively high level, which limits more detailed experts like object-aware or text-aware.
    *   **Limited Comparison:** The comparison with SOTA only includes methods which can be openly used, so the performance may not be the best compared to closed-source models.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper could influence future research in MLLMs by:

    *   Encouraging the exploration of specialized vision encoders for different tasks.
    *   Promoting the use of efficient mixture-of-experts architectures.
    *   Inspiring the development of more adaptive routing mechanisms.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of MLLMs by addressing a practical problem (domain conflicts) with a novel and efficient architecture (Mixpert).  The strong experimental results, comprehensive ablation studies, and ease of integration with existing models contribute to its significance. While the manual domain definition and limited experiments in the field might constrain its applicability in certain situations, the work presents a sensible method for visual information analysis in existing MLLMs.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Cross-Attention Speculative Decoding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24544v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Zhong, Manasa Bharadwaj, Yixiao Wang, Nikhil Verma, Yipeng Ji, Chul Lee
- **Abstract**: Speculative decoding (SD) is a widely adopted approach for accelerating inference in large language models (LLMs), particularly when the draft and target models are well aligned. However, state-of-the-art SD methods typically rely on tightly coupled, self-attention-based Transformer decoders, often augmented with auxiliary pooling or fusion layers. This coupling makes them increasingly complex and harder to generalize across different models. We present Budget EAGLE (Beagle), the first, to our knowledge, cross-attention-based Transformer decoder SD model that achieves performance on par with leading self-attention SD models (EAGLE-v2) while eliminating the need for pooling or auxiliary components, simplifying the architecture, improving training efficiency, and maintaining stable memory usage during training-time simulation. To enable effective training of this novel architecture, we propose Two-Stage Block-Attention Training, a new method that achieves training stability and convergence efficiency in block-level attention scenarios. Extensive experiments across multiple LLMs and datasets show that Beagle achieves competitive inference speedups and higher training efficiency than EAGLE-v2, offering a strong alternative for architectures in speculative decoding.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Budget EAGLE (Beagle), a novel speculative decoding (SD) method for large language models (LLMs). Unlike many existing SD approaches that rely on tightly coupled, self-attention-based Transformer decoders (often with auxiliary components), Beagle uses a cross-attention-based Transformer decoder, simplifying the architecture. The authors propose a Two-Stage Block-Attention Training method to effectively train this architecture, addressing training stability and convergence in block-level attention scenarios. The results indicate that Beagle achieves competitive inference speedups and higher training efficiency than EAGLE-v2 (a leading self-attention SD model), while maintaining stable memory usage.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the architecture: a cross-attention-based Transformer decoder for speculative decoding that removes auxiliary components. The Two-Stage Block-Attention Training is also a novel approach specifically designed for this architecture.
*   **Significance:** The significance comes from the simplification of the SD architecture, potentially making it easier to integrate into different models and frameworks. The improved training efficiency is also a noteworthy contribution, as it can reduce the cost of training SD models. The constant memory usage for training makes large model training possible on lower-end hardware. However, the performance is only on par with EAGLE-v2, which limits the impact.

**Strengths:**

*   **Simpler Architecture:** The cross-attention-based approach eliminates the need for auxiliary layers and copying of states, simplifying the model and potentially improving memory locality.
*   **Improved Training Efficiency:** The Two-Stage Block-Attention Training method achieves stable and efficient training.
*   **Competitive Performance:** Beagle demonstrates performance on par with a leading self-attention SD model (EAGLE-v2).
*   **Strong Evaluations:** Comprehensive experiments across multiple LLMs and datasets validate the effectiveness of the proposed method.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Performance Gains Limited:** While Beagle is competitive with EAGLE-v2, it doesn't significantly outperform it. It essentially provides an *alternative* architecture, not a superior one in terms of pure performance.
*   **Incremental Improvement on EAGLE-v2:** Although technically distinct in its architecture and training scheme, a large portion of the work is centered on improved training performance that is only marginally better than the already very efficient EAGLE architecture.
*   **Limited Evaluation Scope:** The focus on single-batch greedy decoding is somewhat limiting. An evaluation in speculative sampling could add more weight to the practicality of the results.
*   **Dependence on EAGLE settings**: Replicating settings of EAGLE-v2, while useful for fair comparison, potentially biases the evaluations in favor of this architecture, making the gains less impressive.

**Potential Influence:**

The work could influence the design of SD models by demonstrating that cross-attention can be a viable alternative to self-attention, particularly when combined with a tailored training strategy. The simplified architecture may encourage wider adoption of SD in different LLM inference frameworks. The new training technique allows faster experimentation with different model design variations.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel architecture for speculative decoding with improved training efficiency. However, the lack of significant performance gains over EAGLE-v2, the already efficient training technique of EAGLE-v2, and the limited evaluation scope temper the overall impact. The work is a good contribution to the field but not a major breakthrough.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### A*-Thought: Efficient Reasoning via Bidirectional Compression for Low-Resource Settings
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24550v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaoang Xu, Shuo Wang, Xu Han, Zhenghao Liu, Huijia Wu, Peipei Li, Zhiyuan Liu, Maosong Sun, Zhaofeng He
- **Abstract**: Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) achieve superior performance by extending the thought length. However, a lengthy thinking trajectory leads to reduced efficiency. Most of the existing methods are stuck in the assumption of overthinking and attempt to reason efficiently by compressing the Chain-of-Thought, but this often leads to performance degradation. To address this problem, we introduce A*-Thought, an efficient tree search-based unified framework designed to identify and isolate the most essential thoughts from the extensive reasoning chains produced by these models. It formulates the reasoning process of LRMs as a search tree, where each node represents a reasoning span in the giant reasoning space. By combining the A* search algorithm with a cost function specific to the reasoning path, it can efficiently compress the chain of thought and determine a reasoning path with high information density and low cost. In addition, we also propose a bidirectional importance estimation mechanism, which further refines this search process and enhances its efficiency beyond uniform sampling. Extensive experiments on several advanced math tasks show that A*-Thought effectively balances performance and efficiency over a huge search space. Specifically, A*-Thought can improve the performance of QwQ-32B by 2.39$\times$ with low-budget and reduce the length of the output token by nearly 50% with high-budget. The proposed method is also compatible with several other LRMs, demonstrating its generalization capability. The code can be accessed at: https://github.com/AI9Stars/AStar-Thought.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "A*-Thought: Efficient Reasoning via Bidirectional Compression for Low-Resource Settings":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces A*-Thought, a novel framework designed to improve the efficiency of large reasoning models (LRMs) in low-resource settings. The core idea is to compress the typically lengthy "chain of thought" (CoT) reasoning process of LRMs by identifying and retaining only the most essential thoughts. The framework formulates the reasoning process as a search tree, where each node represents a reasoning step. It utilizes the A* search algorithm combined with a cost function that balances path quality and conditional self-information. A key component is a bidirectional importance estimation mechanism (BIS) that assesses the significance of each thought based on its relevance to both the question and the solution. The authors demonstrate empirically that A*-Thought improves the performance and efficiency of LRMs on mathematical reasoning tasks, showing improvements in accuracy, reduced output length, and compatibility with various LRM architectures.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the integration of the A* search algorithm with a bidirectional importance estimation score (BIS) for CoT compression. While A* search and CoT compression are not entirely new concepts, the combination and the specific design of the BIS and cost functions appears to be a novel contribution. The BIS, in particular, seems to offer a differentiated approach compared to existing methods for token or thought importance estimation. The way it combines attention and model levels in the importance scoring is also a positive feature.
* **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant problem in the field: the high computational cost of LRMs, particularly when deployed in resource-constrained environments. The method has a direct relevance to real-world applications, where reducing inference cost is crucial. The experimental results, showing substantial accuracy improvements and length reductions, demonstrate the potential of the proposed approach. Claimed compatibility of the proposed model is another potential benefit.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Well-defined problem and solution:**  The paper clearly articulates the problem of CoT redundancy and provides a well-structured solution using A* search and BIS.
    * **Comprehensive experimental evaluation:** The experiments cover a range of mathematical reasoning tasks and compare against multiple baselines. The improvements demonstrated by A*-Thought are compelling.
    * **Empirical results:** The paper demonstrates a significant accuracy improvements across multiple tasks. A* algorithm in its modified form also leads to considerable decrease in the output length.
    * **Compatibility with multiple LRMs:** The paper demonstrates the A*-Thought's compatibility across multiple models: QwQ-32B, R1-Distill-32B and s1.1-32B.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on GPT-2 for BIS:** The use of GPT-2 for the BIS calculation raises questions about the computational overhead of this process, which might partially offset the gains from CoT compression. The authors claim it enhances efficiency, but further analysis of the runtime cost of BIS vs. reduction in CoT length would make the result more convincing.
    * **Limited scope of experiments:** The evaluation focuses primarily on mathematical reasoning tasks. While these are challenging, it would strengthen the paper to include experiments on other reasoning tasks, such as commonsense reasoning or logical inference.
    * **Hyperparameter sensitivity:** The performance of A*-Thought is likely sensitive to the hyperparameters (α, β, kmin, kmax, and W). While the paper mentions the default values, a more thorough ablation study could explore the sensitivity of these parameters and provide guidelines for setting them in different scenarios.
    * **Lack of analysis and limitations:** Although model is supposed to be compatible with multiple LRMs, there is limited experiments with models apart from QwQ-32B.

* **Potential Influence:** If the approach proves robust across diverse tasks and can be implemented efficiently, it could influence the development of more efficient and deployable LRMs. The BIS mechanism might inspire other researchers to explore bidirectional importance estimation techniques. The open-sourced code will definitely help to further explore this model and expand the scope of research in this field.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and potentially significant method for improving the efficiency of LRMs. The experimental results are compelling, demonstrating substantial improvements over baselines. However, the weaknesses outlined above, particularly the GPT-2 overhead and the limited scope of the experiments, prevent it from achieving a higher score. The reliance on a pre-trained LM for estimating BIS limits the applicability of this method to other settings. Overall, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses, but the paper could benefit from further investigation and validation.
