### Revisiting Epistemic Markers in Confidence Estimation: Can Markers Accurately Reflect Large Language Models' Uncertainty?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24778v1)
- **Authors**: Jiayu Liu, Qing Zong, Weiqi Wang, Yangqiu Song
- **Abstract**: As large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used in high-stakes domains, accurately assessing their confidence is crucial. Humans typically express confidence through epistemic markers (e.g., "fairly confident") instead of numerical values. However, it remains unclear whether LLMs consistently use these markers to reflect their intrinsic confidence due to the difficulty of quantifying uncertainty associated with various markers. To address this gap, we first define marker confidence as the observed accuracy when a model employs an epistemic marker. We evaluate its stability across multiple question-answering datasets in both in-distribution and out-of-distribution settings for open-source and proprietary LLMs. Our results show that while markers generalize well within the same distribution, their confidence is inconsistent in out-of-distribution scenarios. These findings raise significant concerns about the reliability of epistemic markers for confidence estimation, underscoring the need for improved alignment between marker based confidence and actual model uncertainty. Our code is available at https://github.com/HKUST-KnowComp/MarCon.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the reliability of Large Language Models (LLMs) using epistemic markers (e.g., "fairly certain") to express confidence in their answers to question-answering tasks. The core idea is to define "marker confidence" as the accuracy achieved when a model uses a specific epistemic marker.  The authors evaluate this marker confidence across several open-source and proprietary LLMs on multiple datasets, both in-distribution and out-of-distribution, by examining whether LLMs consistently use these markers to reflect intrinsic confidence. They propose several metrics to analyze the stability and consistency of marker usage. Their key findings suggest that while LLMs might exhibit stable marker confidence within a specific data distribution, this consistency deteriorates significantly in out-of-distribution scenarios, raising concerns about the reliability of these markers for high-stakes applications. They also noted that more powerful models seem to exhibit a better understanding of epistemic markers than smaller models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper addresses a relevant problem within the field of LLM trustworthiness: how well models convey uncertainty using natural language. While previous works have examined LLMs' ability to *interpret* such markers (from humans), this paper's focus on LLMs' ability to *reliably generate* and *consistently use* them to express confidence is a valuable contribution.  The definition of "marker confidence" and the suite of metrics designed to evaluate consistency are also novel and provide a structured way to analyze this problem. However, The conclusion in the paper are not ground breaking or particularly surprising since it is already known that LLM’s behavior is unstable and can be easily manipulated, even when it comes to expressing certainty or providing correct answers.

**Significance:**  Accurate confidence estimation is crucial for deploying LLMs in real-world applications, especially in high-stakes domains like healthcare or law.  If models are overconfident in their incorrect answers, it can lead to serious consequences.  By demonstrating the instability of epistemic marker usage across different data distributions, this paper highlights a significant limitation of current LLMs. The finding that more powerful models display a better understanding of epistemic markers could lead to future research direction. The emphasis on intrinsic marker confidence rather than comparison to human interpretation also contributes meaningfully to understanding and improving LLM reliability. The evaluation metrics proposed in the paper could be useful for other researchers to evaluate LLM stability.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of unreliable epistemic marker usage in LLMs.
*   **Rigorous Methodology:** The authors employ a comprehensive experimental setup, evaluating a range of models and datasets.
*   **Novel Metrics:**  The proposed metrics, particularly I-AvgECE, C-AvgECE, MAC, and MRC, provide a structured way to assess marker stability and consistency.
*   **Practical Implications:** The findings have clear implications for the deployment of LLMs in real-world applications, highlighting the need for more robust confidence estimation techniques.
*   **Well-organized and well-written:** The paper has a logical flow, starting with problem definition and concluding with experimental results.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Simplified Definition of Confidence:**  The paper's definition of "marker confidence" focuses solely on accuracy. It does not consider nuanced understanding of confidence associated with epistemic markers, for example, if models are using a marker correctly but are more or less confident than they should be (degree of epistemic marker).
*   **Limited Scope of Epistemic Markers:** The study could benefit from a more comprehensive analysis of a wider range of epistemic markers, exploring different types of uncertainty and degrees of confidence.
*   **Lack of explanation:** The paper doesn't explain very well why certain models show more ability to differentiate markers. Without this, it is harder to derive actionable insights or guidance.
*   **Limited Dataset:** The chosen datasets are predominantly question-answering tasks. While informative, they may not fully capture the nuances of language used to express confidence in other types of LLM applications (e.g., creative writing, summarization).
*   **Over Claims:**  While the paper establishes important findings, it needs to avoid overstating them. Specifically, it shouldn’t be claimed that current LLMs do not really *understand* epistemic markers, when such understanding is difficult to define or establish empirically.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the identified strengths and weaknesses, I assign a score of **6**. The paper makes a valuable contribution by highlighting the instability of epistemic markers in LLMs and provides a novel framework and metrics for evaluating this phenomenon. However, it suffers from limitations in the scope of the analyzed markers and datasets. The work also is not ground breaking nor does it include substantial insight into why LLMs behave as they do. Therefore, while the work presents a meaningful contribution to the field of LLM reliability, its impact is somewhat limited by the above.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### QGAN-based data augmentation for hybrid quantum-classical neural networks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24780v1)
- **Authors**: Run-Ze He, Jun-Jian Su, Su-Juan Qin, Zheng-Ping Jin, Fei Gao
- **Abstract**: Quantum neural networks converge faster and achieve higher accuracy than classical models. However, data augmentation in quantum machine learning remains underexplored. To tackle data scarcity, we integrate quantum generative adversarial networks (QGANs) with hybrid quantum-classical neural networks (HQCNNs) to develop an augmentation framework. We propose two strategies: a general approach to enhance data processing and classification across HQCNNs, and a customized strategy that dynamically generates samples tailored to the HQCNN's performance on specific data categories, improving its ability to learn from complex datasets. Simulation experiments on the MNIST dataset demonstrate that QGAN outperforms traditional data augmentation methods and classical GANs. Compared to baseline DCGAN, QGAN achieves comparable performance with half the parameters, balancing efficiency and effectiveness. This suggests that QGANs can simplify models and generate high-quality data, enhancing HQCNN accuracy and performance. These findings pave the way for applying quantum data augmentation techniques in machine learning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a data augmentation framework using Quantum Generative Adversarial Networks (QGANs) to address data scarcity in Hybrid Quantum-Classical Neural Networks (HQCNNs). The authors introduce two augmentation strategies: a general-purpose strategy for overall performance enhancement and a customized strategy that targets specific data categories based on the HQCNN's performance. They present simulation results on the MNIST dataset demonstrating QGAN's superiority over traditional data augmentation methods and classical GANs, showing comparable performance to DCGANs with fewer parameters. The paper argues that QGANs can simplify models, generate high-quality data, and improve HQCNN accuracy, suggesting a promising avenue for quantum data augmentation in machine learning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using QGANs for data augmentation in HQCNNs possesses a degree of novelty. The integration of quantum generative models with hybrid quantum-classical networks is a relatively unexplored area. However, the conceptual framework of GAN-based data augmentation itself isn't entirely new, as classical GANs have been widely used for this purpose. The specific application to HQCNNs and the proposed customized augmentation strategy contribute to the novelty, though the novelty is somewhat incremental rather than fundamentally disruptive.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in quantum machine learning: data scarcity. Overcoming this limitation is essential for the practical application of quantum machine learning algorithms. If the proposed QGAN-based augmentation proves effective and scalable, it could significantly accelerate the development and deployment of HQCNNs. Furthermore, showcasing QGANs' efficiency in terms of parameter count compared to classical GANs highlights potential quantum advantages in generative modeling.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper presents a clear problem statement and motivation.
    *   The two proposed augmentation strategies are well-defined and distinct.
    *   The simulation experiments on MNIST provide empirical evidence to support the claims.
    *   The comparison with classical GANs and traditional data augmentation techniques strengthens the analysis.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The experimental validation is limited to the MNIST dataset. The results might not generalize to more complex, high-dimensional datasets.
    *   The paper lacks a detailed discussion on the computational complexity and scalability of the proposed QGAN-based augmentation. Simulating large-scale quantum systems is known to be computationally expensive, and the practicality of the approach needs to be rigorously evaluated.
    *   The paper doesn't extensively discuss the impact of different QGAN architectures or the choice of quantum circuits on the augmentation performance.
    *   While QGANs are compared to traditional data augmentation techniques and classical GANs, there is limited theoretical analysis regarding the properties of QGAN-generated data and its relationship to the training data. This lacks a rigorous mathematical understanding of why QGANs are claimed to be advantageous.
*   **Potential Impact:** If the approach can be scaled and generalized to more complex datasets, it could have a significant impact on the field of quantum machine learning. Overcoming data scarcity is crucial for realizing the potential of quantum machine learning algorithms. The potential advantage of QGANs in terms of parameter efficiency is also a valuable contribution. However, the paper needs to demonstrate its effectiveness beyond MNIST and address scalability concerns to fully realize its potential impact.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper introduces a promising approach to data augmentation in HQCNNs using QGANs, its novelty is incremental and the experimental validation is limited. The potential impact is significant, but hinges on addressing the scalability concerns and providing a more rigorous theoretical understanding. Therefore, a score of 6 reflects the paper's contribution.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Draw ALL Your Imagine: A Holistic Benchmark and Agent Framework for Complex Instruction-based Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24787v1)
- **Authors**: Yucheng Zhou, Jiahao Yuan, Qianning Wang
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in text-to-image (T2I) generation have enabled models to produce high-quality images from textual descriptions. However, these models often struggle with complex instructions involving multiple objects, attributes, and spatial relationships. Existing benchmarks for evaluating T2I models primarily focus on general text-image alignment and fail to capture the nuanced requirements of complex, multi-faceted prompts. Given this gap, we introduce LongBench-T2I, a comprehensive benchmark specifically designed to evaluate T2I models under complex instructions. LongBench-T2I consists of 500 intricately designed prompts spanning nine diverse visual evaluation dimensions, enabling a thorough assessment of a model's ability to follow complex instructions. Beyond benchmarking, we propose an agent framework (Plan2Gen) that facilitates complex instruction-driven image generation without requiring additional model training. This framework integrates seamlessly with existing T2I models, using large language models to interpret and decompose complex prompts, thereby guiding the generation process more effectively. As existing evaluation metrics, such as CLIPScore, fail to adequately capture the nuances of complex instructions, we introduce an evaluation toolkit that automates the quality assessment of generated images using a set of multi-dimensional metrics. The data and code are released at https://github.com/yczhou001/LongBench-T2I.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LongBench-T2I, a new benchmark for evaluating text-to-image (T2I) models on complex, long-form instructions.  It consists of 500 prompts designed to assess nine visual dimensions. Beyond the benchmark, the authors propose Plan2Gen, an agent framework that uses large language models (LLMs) to parse complex instructions and guide image generation without requiring additional model training.  The framework decomposes the scene into background, midground, and foreground, then iteratively generates and refines each layer. The authors also present an evaluation toolkit that automates quality assessment using multi-dimensional metrics. They compare various closed-source and open-source models, along with diffusion and autoregressive architectures, using LongBench-T2I and demonstrate the effectiveness of Plan2Gen.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper offers novelty in two main aspects: the benchmark and the agent framework.  LongBench-T2I addresses a real gap in existing T2I benchmarks, which often fail to capture the nuances of complex, multi-faceted instructions.  The nine visual dimensions considered are well-defined and provide a more holistic evaluation than simpler benchmarks. Plan2Gen's approach of using LLMs for scene decomposition and iterative refinement is also a novel application of language models in T2I. The agent based approach is not entirely new to the field, however. The originality comes down to the proposed workflow.

* **Significance:** The creation of a robust benchmark for complex instructions is highly significant.  As T2I models become more capable, there's an increasing need for benchmarks that can accurately assess their ability to follow detailed and nuanced prompts. LongBench-T2I could become a standard tool for evaluating and comparing T2I models, pushing the field towards more controllable and user-aligned generation.  The Plan2Gen framework is significant because it offers a way to improve image generation from complex instructions without requiring model retraining. This makes it accessible to a wide range of users and potentially accelerates progress in T2I. The authors do acknowledge that a major issue exists in that the evaluation metrics like CLIPScore may not capture the nuances of the prompts given and introduces a new evaluation toolkit to address the issue.

* **Strengths:**
    * The paper clearly identifies a limitation in current T2I benchmarks.
    * The LongBench-T2I dataset and evaluation dimensions are well-motivated and comprehensive.
    * The Plan2Gen framework is a practical approach to improving complex instruction-based image generation.
    * The experimental results are thorough, comparing a wide range of models.
    * The paper is well-written and clearly explains the methodology.
    * The release of the data and code enhances reproducibility and encourages further research.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * The reliance on LLMs for evaluation, while addressing a gap, introduces potential biases. The LLM's interpretation of "correctness" may not always align with human preferences.
    * While the paper highlights Plan2Gen's performance, it is not entirely clear what each model component of the agent contributes. An ablation study of each component would be beneficial.
    * The current human evaluation is a bit limited.  More comprehensive human studies (e.g., with more participants and a broader range of prompts) would further strengthen the paper's claims.
    * The experiments were run based on Gemini models, which are closed-source. The experiments may not be 100% reproducible.

* **Potential Impact:** This paper has the potential to significantly impact the T2I field by providing a more rigorous benchmark and a practical framework for generating images from complex instructions. It can foster research into improving the controllability and user-alignment of T2I models, leading to more creative and versatile image generation systems.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of 7.5. The creation of a novel benchmark for complex text-to-image generation, LongBench-T2I, addresses a crucial gap. However, the paper faces potential biases and relies on large language models for quality assessments which are potentially subjective. In addition, the novelty of the approach is not completely clear due to the common usage of agent based frameworks in existing research. Overall, there is some novelty and significant impact within the field.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Guiding Generative Storytelling with Knowledge Graphs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24803v1)
- **Authors**: Zhijun Pan, Antonios Andronis, Eva Hayek, Oscar AP Wilkinson, Ilya Lasy, Annette Parry, Guy Gadney, Tim J. Smith, Mick Grierson
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown great potential in automated story generation, but challenges remain in maintaining long-form coherence and providing users with intuitive and effective control. Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) has proven effective in reducing hallucinations in text generation; however, the use of structured data to support generative storytelling remains underexplored. This paper investigates how knowledge graphs (KGs) can enhance LLM-based storytelling by improving narrative quality and enabling user-driven modifications. We propose a KG-assisted storytelling pipeline and evaluate its effectiveness through a user study with 15 participants. Participants created their own story prompts, generated stories, and edited knowledge graphs to shape their narratives. Through quantitative and qualitative analysis, our findings demonstrate that knowledge graphs significantly enhance story quality in action-oriented and structured narratives within our system settings. Additionally, editing the knowledge graph increases users' sense of control, making storytelling more engaging, interactive, and playful.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores using knowledge graphs (KGs) to enhance long-form story generation with Large Language Models (LLMs).  It addresses the challenges of maintaining coherence and providing user control in LLM-based storytelling. The authors propose a KG-assisted storytelling pipeline where LLMs and KGs interact bidirectionally.  A user study involving 15 participants was conducted, where they created story prompts, generated stories, and edited KGs to shape narratives. The results suggest that KGs improve story quality, particularly in action-oriented narratives, and that editing the KG increases user agency and engagement.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using knowledge graphs to improve long-form coherence in LLM-based storytelling is not entirely novel.  Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) and Graph RAG have been explored, and the use of structured knowledge has been touched on in previous works as cited by the authors. The paper's contribution lies in the specific implementation of the KG-LLM pipeline, the focus on long-form narratives (compared to shorter text generation tasks), and the emphasis on user control through editable KGs. However, the novelty is somewhat incremental rather than a paradigm shift.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its practical investigation of how editable KGs can enhance user agency and creative control in storytelling. The user study provides empirical evidence that editing KGs increases user control, making storytelling more engaging, interactive, and playful, particularly for action-oriented stories. The identification of limitations with introspective narratives is also a useful contribution, pointing to avenues for future research. However, the relatively small sample size (N=15) and the limited diversity of participants restrict the generalizability of the findings.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition: The paper addresses relevant challenges in LLM-based storytelling.
    *   Well-defined pipeline: The architecture of the KG-assisted pipeline is clearly explained.
    *   User study: The inclusion of a user study provides valuable insights into the user experience and the impact of KG editing.
    *   Analysis of different narrative types: The distinction between action-oriented and introspective narratives highlights the nuanced effectiveness of KGs.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited novelty: As noted, the core concept builds upon existing RAG approaches.
    *   Small sample size: The small number of participants weakens the statistical power and generalizability of the study.
    *   Model choice: The decision to use a smaller 8B Llama model is justifiable but limits the potential performance of the pipeline and might have exacerbated the inconsistencies noted in some narratives. Using Llama also limits the possibilities of prompt engineering, which is an area to improve the LLM response.
    *   Lack of a strong baseline: Although the study did compare knowledge graph enabled and non-knowledge-graph enabled stories, the baseline was fairly simplistic. Including alternative baseline methods that attempt to improve coherence and user control could strengthen the paper.
    *   Limited Interface: The paper states it did not include an advanced UI due to RQ2 focusing on the knowledge graph effectiveness. However, this adds difficulty on users, and might skew results.
    *   Limited comparison.
    *   The statistical significance is lacking in some of the analysis.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings could influence future research in interactive storytelling systems, particularly those aiming to empower users with creative control. The identification of narrative type-specific limitations could guide the development of more targeted KG-based approaches. The focus on playfulness and engagement highlights the importance of user experience considerations in the design of AI-assisted creative tools.

**Score and Justification:**

Considering the paper's incremental novelty, limitations of the experiment, and potential influence, a score of **6** is appropriate. The paper presents a well-defined and investigated approach to enhance LLM storytelling, making a useful contribution to the field. However, the limitations restrict the conclusions that can be drawn. Future work with more extensive experiments, stronger baselines, larger and more varied datasets and participants, and further investigation of narrative type-specific approaches is necessary to solidify the findings and fully realize the potential of this approach.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### RealDrive: Retrieval-Augmented Driving with Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24808v1)
- **Authors**: Wenhao Ding, Sushant Veer, Yuxiao Chen, Yulong Cao, Chaowei Xiao, Marco Pavone
- **Abstract**: Learning-based planners generate natural human-like driving behaviors by learning to reason about nuanced interactions from data, overcoming the rigid behaviors that arise from rule-based planners. Nonetheless, data-driven approaches often struggle with rare, safety-critical scenarios and offer limited controllability over the generated trajectories. To address these challenges, we propose RealDrive, a Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) framework that initializes a diffusion-based planning policy by retrieving the most relevant expert demonstrations from the training dataset. By interpolating between current observations and retrieved examples through a denoising process, our approach enables fine-grained control and safe behavior across diverse scenarios, leveraging the strong prior provided by the retrieved scenario. Another key insight we produce is that a task-relevant retrieval model trained with planning-based objectives results in superior planning performance in our framework compared to a task-agnostic retriever. Experimental results demonstrate improved generalization to long-tail events and enhanced trajectory diversity compared to standard learning-based planners -- we observe a 40% reduction in collision rate on the Waymo Open Motion dataset with RAG.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "RealDrive: Retrieval-Augmented Driving with Diffusion Models" introduces a novel framework (RealDrive) to enhance learning-based autonomous driving planners.  RealDrive leverages a Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) approach, initializing a diffusion-based planning policy by retrieving the most relevant expert demonstrations from a training dataset. By interpolating between current observations and retrieved examples via a denoising process, the method achieves fine-grained control and improves safety across diverse scenarios. A key contribution is the task-relevant retrieval model trained with planning-based objectives, leading to superior planning performance compared to task-agnostic retrievers.  Experiments on the Waymo Open Motion dataset demonstrate improved generalization to rare events and enhanced trajectory diversity, resulting in a significant (40%) reduction in collision rates.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The integration of RAG with diffusion models for autonomous driving *is* a significant step, particularly focusing on low-level trajectory planning. While RAG has been used with LLMs in this domain, the paper carves out novelty by using a task-specific embedding model *trained with trajectory planning objectives*, rather than relying on general-purpose language or vision embeddings. The retrieval interpolation module (RIM) is another novel aspect, specifically designed to blend retrieved behaviors with current observations.  The direct use of retrieved trajectories as initial conditions for the diffusion model is a solid contribution as well. The comparison to existing methods like READ and RAGDP, and the differentiation that RealDrive addresses the limitations with the observations retrieved.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in addressing the critical challenges of learning-based planners: generalization to long-tail scenarios and limited controllability.  The 40% reduction in collision rate reported on the Waymo Open Motion dataset is a substantial improvement, suggesting a tangible impact on safety.  The experiments are well-designed, including ablation studies to assess the impact of various components. Furthermore, the qualitative analysis provides valuable insights into how RAG influences planning behavior.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and well-motivated approach.
    *   Novel integration of RAG and diffusion models with task-specific retrieval.
    *   Extensive experiments and ablation studies on standard datasets (nuScenes and Waymo).
    *   Significant performance improvements, particularly in safety (collision rate).
    *   Thorough analysis of the factors influencing performance, providing valuable design guidelines.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Computational cost of maintaining and searching a large retrieval dataset, though the paper provides analysis regarding retrieval latency and the RAG framework.
    *   The reliance on vectorized inputs could limit the applicability to scenarios where raw sensor data (e.g., video) is the primary input.  While acknowledged in the conclusion, this aspect could have been explored further.
    *   The reliance on kinematic bicycle model and inverse dynamics model.
    *   The work also has assumptions based on a fixed-sized vector for scenario embedding and might not scale well with dynamic environments.
    *   The sensitivity to adversarial attacks on the retrieval database is mentioned but not investigated experimentally.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in learning-based autonomous driving, particularly in areas such as:

    *   Development of more sophisticated retrieval models for planning.
    *   Integration of RAG with other planning frameworks (e.g., reinforcement learning).
    *   Addressing the computational challenges of retrieval-augmented planning.
    *   Investigating the robustness of RAG against adversarial attacks.

*   **Conclusion:** Overall, the paper presents a well-executed and significant contribution to the field. The combination of RAG and diffusion models, the task-specific retrieval model, and the substantial performance improvements justify a high score. While there are some limitations, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses.
    *   The use of the sigmoid interpolation scheduler.

### PhySense: Principle-Based Physics Reasoning Benchmarking for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24823v1)
- **Authors**: Yinggan Xu, Yue Liu, Zhiqiang Gao, Changnan Peng, Di Luo
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have rapidly advanced and are increasingly capable of tackling complex scientific problems, including those in physics. Despite this progress, current LLMs often fail to emulate the concise, principle-based reasoning characteristic of human experts, instead generating lengthy and opaque solutions. This discrepancy highlights a crucial gap in their ability to apply core physical principles for efficient and interpretable problem solving. To systematically investigate this limitation, we introduce PhySense, a novel principle-based physics reasoning benchmark designed to be easily solvable by experts using guiding principles, yet deceptively difficult for LLMs without principle-first reasoning. Our evaluation across multiple state-of-the-art LLMs and prompt types reveals a consistent failure to align with expert-like reasoning paths, providing insights for developing AI systems with efficient, robust and interpretable principle-based scientific reasoning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "PhySense: Principle-Based Physics Reasoning Benchmarking for Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PhySense, a new benchmark dataset designed to assess the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to perform principle-based physics reasoning. The dataset comprises 380 physics problems that are designed to be easily solvable by physicists leveraging fundamental principles like symmetry, conservation laws, and dimensional analysis, but more challenging for LLMs if they rely on brute-force computation or complex reasoning chains. The authors evaluate a range of state-of-the-art LLMs using zero-shot, hint-based, and computation-restricted prompts, demonstrating that LLMs consistently struggle to emulate the efficient, principle-driven reasoning of human experts.  The results suggest that LLMs often resort to lengthy, opaque solutions, highlighting a gap in their ability to effectively apply core physical principles for problem-solving. The paper concludes by emphasizing the need for developing LLMs that are more efficient, robust, and interpretable in their scientific reasoning.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of the paper lies primarily in the design and creation of the PhySense benchmark. While existing physics benchmarks focus on broad domains or computationally intensive problems, PhySense uniquely targets principle-based reasoning. This focused approach fills a gap in the evaluation of LLMs for scientific applications. The explicit focus on expert-like "shortcuts" using first principles is a valuable contribution.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its ability to expose a critical weakness in current LLMs: the lack of efficient, interpretable reasoning based on fundamental physical principles. This deficiency undermines the trustworthiness and applicability of LLMs in scientific contexts where explainability and conceptual soundness are paramount. The paper convincingly demonstrates that achieving high accuracy on physics problems isn't enough; the *way* LLMs arrive at solutions matters, and current approaches are often far from ideal. The identified over-reliance on computational methods as opposed to principle-based reasoning carries significant weight in understanding how LLMs function and where they need improvement.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined Benchmark:** The PhySense dataset is carefully curated and designed to specifically target principle-based reasoning, filling a clear niche in existing evaluation resources. The examples shown in the paper demonstrates that the dataset truly tests LLM's knowledge of fundamental principles.
    *   **Clear Experimental Design:** The use of various prompting strategies (zero-shot, hint-based, no-computation) allows for a nuanced investigation of LLMs' reasoning capabilities. These different prompting styles truly tests the LLMs' ability to apply these fundamental principles.
    *   **Compelling Results:** The consistent failure of LLMs to align with expert-like reasoning paths provides strong evidence for the identified gap in principle-based reasoning. The observations are consistent across multiple models and prompt variations.
    *   **Clear Articulation of Problem:** The paper provides a clear and compelling argument for the importance of principle-based reasoning in scientific applications.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** While the focus on principle-based reasoning is a strength, the paper could be strengthened by including a more diverse set of physics subfields beyond those currently represented in PhySense.
    *   **Text-Only Input:** The benchmark is exclusively text-based, neglecting multi-modal reasoning that could be relevant in real-world scientific problem-solving (e.g., analyzing diagrams, plots, or experimental data).
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** While accuracy and token usage are appropriate metrics, more sophisticated metrics that directly quantify the *quality* of reasoning could further enhance the evaluation. Metrics that could analyze the individual steps of the LLM's reasoning would better demonstrate whether the principle are applied correctly or whether there are "over-thinking" steps in the process.
    *   **Limited Investigation on Solutions:** The LLM responses aren't analyzed as thoroughly as it could be. Detailed breakdowns of why specific LLMs fail in each of the problems would be insightful.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the development of LLMs for scientific applications by highlighting the need for improved principle-based reasoning capabilities. It could motivate research on new training strategies, model architectures, or evaluation metrics that address the identified limitations. In particular, future works could use this dataset to train better LLMs that do rely on principles.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a well-defined benchmark that addresses a significant gap in the evaluation of LLMs for scientific applications. The evidence demonstrates the LLMs truly don't understand fundamental principles and resort to brute-force computation. The results are compelling and the arguments are clearly articulated. While the paper has some limitations in scope and evaluation metrics, its contribution to the understanding and improvement of LLMs in scientific reasoning is substantial. Overall, it represents a solid advancement.

Score: 7.8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### LegalEval-Q: A New Benchmark for The Quality Evaluation of LLM-Generated Legal Text
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24826v1)
- **Authors**: Li yunhan, Wu gengshen
- **Abstract**: As large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used in legal applications, current evaluation benchmarks tend to focus mainly on factual accuracy while largely neglecting important linguistic quality aspects such as clarity, coherence, and terminology. To address this gap, we propose three steps: First, we develop a regression model to evaluate the quality of legal texts based on clarity, coherence, and terminology. Second, we create a specialized set of legal questions. Third, we analyze 49 LLMs using this evaluation framework. Our analysis identifies three key findings: First, model quality levels off at 14 billion parameters, with only a marginal improvement of $2.7\%$ noted at 72 billion parameters. Second, engineering choices such as quantization and context length have a negligible impact, as indicated by statistical significance thresholds above 0.016. Third, reasoning models consistently outperform base architectures. A significant outcome of our research is the release of a ranking list and Pareto analysis, which highlight the Qwen3 series as the optimal choice for cost-performance tradeoffs. This work not only establishes standardized evaluation protocols for legal LLMs but also uncovers fundamental limitations in current training data refinement approaches. Code and models are available at: https://github.com/lyxx3rd/LegalEval-Q.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "LegalEval-Q: A New Benchmark for The Quality Evaluation of LLM-Generated Legal Text":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LegalEval-Q, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the quality of legal text generated by Large Language Models (LLMs).  The authors argue that existing benchmarks primarily focus on factual accuracy and neglect crucial linguistic aspects like clarity, coherence, and terminology specific to the legal domain.  LegalEval-Q consists of: (1) a regression model that assesses text quality based on clarity, coherence, and terminology, (2) a specialized dataset of legal questions across various subdomains, and (3) an analysis of 49 LLMs using this benchmark.  The key findings include that model quality plateaus at around 14 billion parameters, architectural choices (quantization and context length) have a negligible impact on text quality, and reasoning models consistently outperform base models.  The authors also release a ranking list and Pareto analysis to aid in model selection for legal applications, highlighting the Qwen3 series as an optimal choice for cost-performance tradeoffs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses an important gap:** The paper correctly identifies a significant deficiency in existing LLM evaluation benchmarks – the lack of robust metrics for assessing text quality, especially in specialized domains like law. Focusing on clarity, coherence, and terminology is crucial for practical legal applications.
*   **Comprehensive benchmark:** The LegalEval-Q benchmark appears reasonably comprehensive, including a diverse dataset of legal questions and a multidimensional assessment model. The breakdown of the evaluation into clarity, coherence, and terminology is a sensible approach. The inclusion of comments to evaluate the responses, as well as the use of a regression-based approach for scoring, adds to the robustness of the benchmark.
*   **Extensive Analysis:**  The analysis of 49 LLMs is a valuable contribution. The comparisons and findings regarding scaling behavior, architectural choices, and the performance of reasoning models provide useful insights for practitioners.
*   **Practical Recommendations:**  The Pareto analysis and model ranking list offer practical guidance for selecting LLMs for specific legal applications, taking into account both performance and cost.
*   **Transparency and reproducibility:** The code and models are made available, increasing transparency and enabling future research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Domain-Specific Bias:** The benchmark is specifically tailored to Chinese legal domains, potentially limiting its generalizability to other legal systems and languages.  While the authors acknowledge this as a future direction for research, it's a significant limitation.
*   **Subjectivity in Annotation:**  Despite attempts at standardization, the comment generation and conclusion synthesis processes likely involve some level of subjectivity, even with domain experts. This could introduce bias into the benchmark and affect the reliability of the results.
*   **Limited Scope of Linguistic Features:** While clarity, coherence, and terminology are important, they do not encompass the entire spectrum of linguistic features relevant to legal text quality. For example, aspects such as argumentation strength, logical consistency, and the appropriate use of legal precedent could be considered. The framework could benefit from further expansion to account for these elements.
*   **Statistical Significance:** The paper mentions statistical significance thresholds, but could improve by providing more detailed information about the statistical tests used and results obtained.
*   **Limited Evaluation of Generalization:** The evaluation focuses on a validation set with the same data source distribution as the training set. A more thorough assessment of generalization capabilities could be performed using data from diverse legal domains or jurisdictions.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The primary novelty of this paper lies in the creation of a **specialized benchmark** tailored to assess the linguistic quality of LLM-generated legal text. While previous research has focused on factual accuracy and reasoning in LLMs, LegalEval-Q addresses the previously neglected, yet crucial, factor of textual quality specific to the legal domain. Additionally, the extensive analysis of LLMs, including a Pareto frontier focused on cost-effectiveness, adds a practical element that will likely be useful for practitioners. The discovery that model quality plateaus at a certain point and that engineering choices have a negligible impact is significant, as it might help researchers prioritize alternate, more efficient approaches.

**Overall Assessment:**

LegalEval-Q represents a solid contribution to the field of LLM evaluation, particularly in the context of legal applications. By addressing the critical gap in assessing textual quality, this research offers valuable insights for practitioners and opens up new avenues for future research. While some limitations remain, particularly regarding domain-specific bias and subjectivity, the benchmark's comprehensiveness, extensive analysis, and practical recommendations make it a valuable addition to the literature. The Pareto frontier analysis provides a very practical contribution.

### Improving Reliability and Explainability of Medical Question Answering through Atomic Fact Checking in Retrieval-Augmented LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24830v1)
- **Authors**: Juraj Vladika, Annika Domres, Mai Nguyen, Rebecca Moser, Jana Nano, Felix Busch, Lisa C. Adams, Keno K. Bressem, Denise Bernhardt, Stephanie E. Combs, Kai J. Borm, Florian Matthes, Jan C. Peeken
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) exhibit extensive medical knowledge but are prone to hallucinations and inaccurate citations, which pose a challenge to their clinical adoption and regulatory compliance. Current methods, such as Retrieval Augmented Generation, partially address these issues by grounding answers in source documents, but hallucinations and low fact-level explainability persist. In this work, we introduce a novel atomic fact-checking framework designed to enhance the reliability and explainability of LLMs used in medical long-form question answering. This method decomposes LLM-generated responses into discrete, verifiable units called atomic facts, each of which is independently verified against an authoritative knowledge base of medical guidelines. This approach enables targeted correction of errors and direct tracing to source literature, thereby improving the factual accuracy and explainability of medical Q&A. Extensive evaluation using multi-reader assessments by medical experts and an automated open Q&A benchmark demonstrated significant improvements in factual accuracy and explainability. Our framework achieved up to a 40% overall answer improvement and a 50% hallucination detection rate. The ability to trace each atomic fact back to the most relevant chunks from the database provides a granular, transparent explanation of the generated responses, addressing a major gap in current medical AI applications. This work represents a crucial step towards more trustworthy and reliable clinical applications of LLMs, addressing key prerequisites for clinical application and fostering greater confidence in AI-assisted healthcare.
- **Summary**: Okay, I can provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper based on the OCR text.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework for improving the reliability and explainability of medical question answering (Q&A) systems built using retrieval-augmented large language models (LLMs). The core of the framework is an atomic fact-checking mechanism. The system decomposes LLM-generated responses into individual, verifiable atomic facts. Each fact is then independently checked against an authoritative vector database of medical guideline documents. Incorrect facts are rewritten, and the corrected facts are incorporated back into the overall answer.  The authors evaluated their framework using medical expert assessments and an automated benchmark (AMEGA), demonstrating substantial improvements in factuality and explainability compared to baseline RAG-based LLMs. They also showed that the benefit of the framework was greater for smaller LLMs and identified the Chain-of-Thought prompt as the most effective for verifying atomic facts. Finally, they showed that it had high accuracy across a broad range of different LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The concept of fact-checking in LLMs is not entirely new. The novelty lies in the application of *atomic* fact-checking, specifically within the medical domain, and the complete end-to-end framework built around it. Decomposing answers into granular facts allows for more precise error detection and correction.  The integration with an authoritative medical guideline database is also valuable. While fact-checking approaches exist, their application to complex medical Q&A, with a focus on explainability through source tracing, is a differentiating factor. A significant addition is the rigorous evaluation with both medical expert evaluation and an automated evaluation.

**Significance:**

The paper addresses a critical problem in medical AI: the potential for LLMs to generate inaccurate or misleading information. The proposed framework contributes to improving the trustworthiness and reliability of medical Q&A systems, which is essential for clinical applications. The emphasis on explainability is crucial for building user confidence and enabling clinicians to understand the reasoning behind LLM-generated answers. The finding that the framework is particularly beneficial for smaller LLMs is significant because it opens up possibilities for deploying reliable medical AI solutions in resource-constrained settings or on-premises environments where larger models may not be feasible. The AMEGA evaluation also offers the unique ability to be compared to similar works across a variety of open-source models.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of hallucination and lack of explainability in medical LLMs.
*   **Well-Defined Framework:** The atomic fact-checking framework is well-defined and explained.
*   **Rigorous Evaluation:** The use of both medical expert evaluation and automated benchmarks (AMEGA) strengthens the validity of the results. The inclusion of multiple evaluation datasets, including real-world tumor board cases, adds practical relevance.
*   **Explainability Focus:** The framework's ability to trace facts back to source documents enhances explainability.
*   **Findings on Smaller LLMs:** The identification of benefits for smaller LLMs is an important contribution.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on LLM Generation:** The framework relies on LLM-generated responses, meaning that errors in initial generation can propagate through the pipeline. Though they discuss this in the conclusion, they do not do much to mitigate it.
*   **Scope of Guidelines:** the guidelines may be limited and lack coverage of certain domains of medicine.
*   **Potential for Retrieval Errors:** The effectiveness of the framework depends on the quality of the retrieval from the vector database. Errors in retrieval could lead to incorrect fact verification. This is not deeply explored in the paper, though they mention it briefly.
*   **Small evaluation size:** They had to restrict the size of the Q&A to 120 Q&As in total, limiting the scope of the analysis.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of medical AI by addressing the crucial issues of factuality and explainability in LLM-based Q&A systems. The atomic fact-checking framework offers a practical approach to improving the reliability of these systems, and the findings on smaller LLMs are particularly promising. While there are some limitations related to the reliance on LLM generation and potential retrieval errors, the strengths of the paper outweigh the weaknesses. The rigorous evaluation and focus on explainability make it a significant contribution.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

The paper demonstrates strong novelty in applying atomic fact-checking to the medical domain, coupled with a robust end-to-end framework and rigorous evaluation. It addresses a critical problem and offers a practical solution that has the potential to improve the trustworthiness and reliability of medical AI systems, especially in lower-resource settings. It also compares itself to existing benchmarks in a rigorous way. The limitations, while present, do not significantly detract from the overall value of the contribution. Therefore, a score of 8 reflects a significant and impactful, but not perfectly flawless, contribution to the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### VideoCAD: A Large-Scale Video Dataset for Learning UI Interactions and 3D Reasoning from CAD Software
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24838v1)
- **Authors**: Brandon Man, Ghadi Nehme, Md Ferdous Alam, Faez Ahmed
- **Abstract**: Computer-Aided Design (CAD) is a time-consuming and complex process, requiring precise, long-horizon user interactions with intricate 3D interfaces. While recent advances in AI-driven user interface (UI) agents show promise, most existing datasets and methods focus on short, low-complexity tasks in mobile or web applications, failing to capture the demands of professional engineering tools. In this work, we introduce VideoCAD, the first attempt at engineering UI interaction learning for precision tasks. Specifically, VideoCAD is a large-scale synthetic dataset consisting of over 41K annotated video recordings of CAD operations, generated using an automated framework for collecting high-fidelity UI action data from human-made CAD designs. Compared to existing datasets, VideoCAD offers an order of magnitude higher complexity in UI interaction learning for real-world engineering tasks, having up to a 20x longer time horizon than other datasets. We show two important downstream applications of VideoCAD: learning UI interactions from professional precision 3D CAD tools and a visual question-answering (VQA) benchmark designed to evaluate multimodal large language models' (LLM) spatial reasoning and video understanding abilities. To learn the UI interactions, we propose VideoCADFormer - a state-of-the-art model in learning CAD interactions directly from video, which outperforms multiple behavior cloning baselines. Both VideoCADFormer and the VQA benchmark derived from VideoCAD reveal key challenges in the current state of video-based UI understanding, including the need for precise action grounding, multi-modal and spatial reasoning, and long-horizon dependencies.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VideoCAD, a new large-scale synthetic dataset of over 41,000 annotated video recordings of CAD (Computer-Aided Design) operations generated from human-made CAD designs in Onshape. The dataset aims to address the lack of high-quality, annotated data for training AI agents to interact with complex engineering UI, specifically CAD software, which has longer time horizons and intricate 3D interfaces compared to typical web and mobile applications. The paper demonstrates the utility of VideoCAD through two downstream tasks: UI interaction learning using a proposed VideoCADFormer model, and a visual question-answering (VQA) benchmark (VideoCADQA) designed to evaluate LLMs' spatial reasoning and video understanding abilities in the CAD domain. The results highlight key challenges in current video-based UI understanding, including the need for precise action grounding, multimodal and spatial reasoning, and capturing long-horizon dependencies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The creation of a large-scale dataset for CAD UI interaction is a significant contribution. Existing UI interaction datasets are often focused on simpler tasks in web or mobile environments, making VideoCAD the first major effort to address the specific challenges of CAD software. The use of synthetic data generation, combined with human-authored CAD designs, is a practical approach to overcome the scarcity of real-world annotated CAD interaction data. While synthetic data has limitations, it offers a controlled environment for training and evaluating AI agents. The downstream tasks and the proposed VideoCADFormer model also contribute to the novelty of the paper. VideoCADFormer provides a new architecture suited for CAD UI interactions, and the VideoCADQA provides a new benchmark to evaluate the performance of vision-language models on 3D reasoning tasks in the context of CAD.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of this work is high. CAD software is critical in many engineering domains, and automating or assisting users with AI-driven tools could significantly improve productivity. The VideoCAD dataset provides a much-needed resource for training and evaluating such AI agents. The benchmark addresses the specific challenges of CAD modeling, which differs significantly from traditional UI navigation tasks. By revealing limitations in LLMs' spatial reasoning abilities in precise engineering tasks, the authors help guide future research in this area. The dataset can be used for several potential use cases beyond the ones presented in the paper, such as reinforcement learning for CAD operations, generation of CAD tutorials, and grounding language in CAD actions.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Scale and Complexity:** The dataset's size and the complexity of the CAD operations it captures differentiate it from existing UI interaction datasets. The longer time horizons and the need for precise 3D reasoning present a unique challenge for AI agents.
    *   **Downstream Tasks:** The paper demonstrates the utility of VideoCAD through two compelling downstream tasks: UI interaction learning and a VQA benchmark.
    *   **Clearly Defined Action Space:** The well-defined action space allows for structured learning of UI interactions.
    *   **Open Availability:** The open availability of the dataset and code is crucial for facilitating further research in this area.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Synthetic Data:** The reliance on synthetic data may limit the generalization of AI agents trained on VideoCAD to real-world CAD usage scenarios. Human errors, variations in modeling styles, and subtle nuances in UI interactions are not captured in the synthetic data.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The dataset is currently limited to sketch-extrude workflows in Onshape. While this represents a common workflow, it does not encompass the full range of CAD operations available in professional software.
    *   **Limited LLM Task:** The LLM is only evaluated on static screenshot inputs, instead of a sequential, interactive task, limiting the potential to assess the usability of such LLMs for interactive design support or automation.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence research in AI-driven UI navigation, software automation, and CAD generation. It will serve as a valuable resource for researchers working on developing AI agents for engineering tools. It has the potential to bridge the gap between computer vision, reinforcement learning, and CAD modeling communities.

*   **Justification:** While the use of synthetic data and limited scope are drawbacks, the scale, complexity, and clear application of the dataset to a critical problem domain make this a significant contribution. The VideoCADFormer model provides a state-of-the-art baseline for CAD UI interaction, and the VQA benchmark provides a solid foundation to assess LLMs' spatial reasoning for CAD.
